# INTRODUCTION

---

**J**ATAKACHUNDRIKA <note>The title translates literally as "Moonlight for Natal Astrology."</note> is a handy pamphlet in Sanskrit and signifies "Moonlight for Astrological sciences." It is a work of great suggestiveness and contains really valuable information necessary for a beginner. Adepts in Astrology have much food for reflection here. Prefatory remarks reconciling the principles of Astrology with those of the modern sciences will be out of place in a work of English translation and I would strongly recommend the readers to secure a copy of my *Astrological Self Instructor* and read the splendid scientific Introduction elaborately given by me there. They will be largely benefitted. English translations of Sanskrit works are always poor performances as the spirit and beauty of the original language can never be adequately represented in the English tongue. Still attempts made in this direction ought not to be despised as they tend to give the readers in English some idea of the contents which the Sanskrit book treats of. The English translation of *Jatakachundrika* has already been published completely in the *Astrological Magazine*, Vols.

<summary>This introduction presents the Jatakachundrika as a foundational yet profound Sanskrit astrological text, while the translator acknowledges the inherent difficulties in capturing the original language's spirit in English.</summary>

<keywords>Jatakachundrika, Sanskrit translation, Vedic astrology, Astrological Self Instructor, Astrological Magazine, translation theory</keywords>

<page-num>ii</page-num>
# INTRODUCTION.

I and II and those who go in for those volumes will find this and many other important subjects treated of there. The notes and illustrations given in the *Astrological Magazine* have been carefully collected and valuable additions have been made here with a view to make them complete. The Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus and Saturn are exercising influences on the Earth and all its phenomena and this has been thoroughly demonstrated by the arguments adduced by me in the columns of the *Astrological Magazine*. Without light and heat nothing could exist on this world even for a second and they both come directly from the Sun and indirectly through the reflection of the other planetary bodies. We have only to stand for a few hours in the Sun to feel that we are influenced by his ever active rays. All the planets shine by borrowed light—from the Sun. The Sun is the accredited head of the whole planetary system and without his energy nothing can live on this Earth. The Earth itself is maintained in its due position by the attraction of the solar rays, and is wholly influenced by them. The terrestrial phenomena can have no other apparent source from which they can borrow their vitality. If the planetary influences are admitted as moulding the *plants* <note>original: "flora"</note> and the *animals* <note>original: "fauna"</note> of a country there will be no consistency in saying that astrology is false, and that a knowledge of the working of these influences is useless.

Astrological works were originally composed by the great <term>Maharishis (Great Sages)</term>, Vasista, Parasara, Vyasa, Gargi, Marichi, Attri, and Jaimini. They were persons endowed with the <term>Divine Sight (Devya Drishti)</term> <note>The ability to see beyond the physical realm or into the future</note>. These had no special motives to pass off a series of facts known to them to be utterly false. Such a position is untenable even for a single minute if we learnt how great men the <term>Rishis (Inspired Sages)</term> were. I shall not argue the *advantages* and *disadvantages* <note>original: "pros and cons"</note> about Astrology. The easiest way of

<summary>The author argues for the scientific and spiritual validity of astrology, citing the physical influence of solar rays and the divine authority of the ancient Maharishis. He asserts that if the Sun and planets affect nature, they must logically affect human life as well.</summary>
<keywords>Astrology, Astrological Magazine, Maharishis, solar energy, planetary influence, Vedic sages, Devya Drishti, divine sight</keywords>

# INTRODUCTION.

Convincing oneself about the truths of astrology will be to study the elementary principles of the science and then judge whether they are worthy of devotion or not. All sciences require some application, average intelligence, and great perseverance. They can only be judged after some progress. Here the same course is precisely recommended. If the results are not convincing after a fair trial has been given to it, the science may be neglected as it deserves to be. No science should be rejected off-hand without a fair investigation into its principles. I may however give the educated public an assurance—from my long personal acquaintance with the subject, extending over twenty years—that when people take to the study of the subject of astrology without any bias, they are sure to find valuable truths in it, which will be highly useful to them in their transactions in life. The time they may be required to spend over the astrological studies will be amply remunerated by the intellectual treat they have there and the *certain* knowledge of the future which they will be able to secure. 

To the Hindu <note>The author writes from a late 19th-century perspective where astrology was deeply integrated into religious and social duties.</note> a knowledge of astrology is simply invaluable. His aims are higher and all his acts are regulated with reference to the existence of a nobler life in future. A knowledge of the future can never be despised and remedies taken to improve our present condition in the light of such knowledge, will be highly useful in our practical life. The author of <term>Jatakachundrika</term> <note>A classic Sanskrit text on predictive astrology, specifically focusing on the "Moon-sign" system.</note>, as well as other authors, presupposes some knowledge in the student, and proceeds upon principles well recognised by the astrological writers. There are 12 Zodiacal signs, 27 constellations divided into 4 <term>quarters</term> <note>original: "Padams"; these are the four steps or divisions of a lunar mansion (Nakshatra).</note> each, nine planets and the complications arising therefrom. The Sun, Venus, and Mercury roughly move in each sign in 30 days, Mars moves in 45 days, Moon moves in 2¼ days, Jupiter

<page-num>iv</page-num>
<header>INTRODUCTION.</header>

moves in 360 days, <term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon, often depicted as a dragon's head in ancient astronomy</note> and <term>Kethu</term> <note>The South Node of the Moon, or the dragon's tail</note> in 540 days, and <term>Saturn</term> in 900 days. The various divisions of the signs, the sources of strength or weakness among the planets, and the names and identifying marks of constellations are not given here. They are so easily procurable that any student can find them in the ordinary books sold in the market. The Author seems to have a clear knowledge of <term>Astrology</term>. He first takes the reader through the planetary aspects, then the good and evil which accrue to planets from ownership, and then association and conjunction. He describes *Rajayoga* <note>Literally "Royal Union," referring to planetary combinations that indicate high status, success, and power in life</note>, the combinations for longevity with a summary of the results of the various planets upon the horoscopes of persons born in each of the twelve Zodiacal signs. <term>Rahu</term> and <term>Kethu</term> are shortly introduced and the most successful as well as the most unfortunate periods have also been indicated. I have given full notes and trust they will make the study of this technical subject easy and agreeable. The illustrative horoscopes will be found to be very useful. As my works have already been most heartily welcomed from all parts of the world, I make no further apology for ushering this improved 2nd edition before the sympathetic and appreciative public.

->**B. SURYANARAIN ROW, B.A., M.R.A.S., M.A.S.B.**<-
<note>Member of the Royal Asiatic Society and Member of the Asiatic Society of Bengal</note>

# Auspiciousness
# Moonlight of Natal Astrology
## ॥ Jataka Chandrika ॥

---

->The Upanishadic doctrine, the pure radiance of the Supreme Being;<-
->The one who holds the lute, that great presence with crimson lips, we worship.<-
<note>original: "सिद्धांतमौपनिषदं शुद्धांतंपरेमेष्ठिनः। विणाधरं महत्किंचित् शेाणाधरमुपास्महे" - This is a traditional invocation to Saraswati, the goddess of wisdom.</note>
॥ Verse 1 ॥

---

# ENGLISH TRANSLATION.

---

**Stanza I.**

I invoke the help of that **Glorious Light** which represents the sum total of the knowledge of the **Upanishads regarding the Absolute** <note>original: "Brahmopanishads"</note> whom we call **Saraswati**, the exalted spouse of **Brahma**, who holds the **Veena** (lute), and who is most beautiful (all perfect).

## NOTES.

No author in the <term>Sanskrit</term> <note>The classical language of ancient India</note> language commences his work without first praying to his God and invoking the help of all that is dear to him. This is generally considered necessary for the completion of the work, and also for the good of the students who commence to read it. This is a small work written by **Venkatesa Pandita** son of **Yajneswara Dikshitar** and though short it is excellently written by him. The author expounds some of the best principles of <term>astrology</term> <note>specifically Jyotisha, the traditional Hindu system of astronomy and astrology</note> in the most concise manner possible. **Sanskrit** works are superior to any others in the world, in the singular fact of their being written in poetry and in a strain which enables the student to master the whole of its con-

<summary>This page features the opening invocation of the Jataka Chandrika, a foundational Sanskrit astrological text, dedicating the work to the goddess Saraswati and introducing the author, Venkatesa Pandita.</summary>
<keywords>Jataka Chandrika, Sanskrit astrology, Saraswati, Venkatesa Pandita, Upanishads, Hindu prayer, Jyotisha</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's introduction to the Jatakachundrika, focusing on the oral tradition of Indian scholarship and the symbolic significance of the goddess Saraswati.</meta>

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

contents without the slightest difficulty. <term>Pundits</term> <note>Scholars or learned masters of specialized knowledge</note> carry all their splendid libraries in their small physical heads and this, when properly used, is the best way of storing knowledge and using it to the greatest advantage. <term>Sanscrit</term> <note>Sanskrit: the ancient liturgical and philosophical language of India</note> Poetry has a peculiar charm which enables the student to remember the subject with great facility and quote it without the slightest exertion. The English translation I have given is not literal, but liberal and the sanscrit text inserted in the <term>Devanagari</term> <note>The script used for writing Sanskrit and Hindi</note> character will help those who know that beautiful language to compare the original text with my translation. I have largely added notes with a view to make the text clear and free from vague or obscure passages. Saraswati represents all knowledge and all *gnana* <note>original: "gnana" — spiritual or religious wisdom</note>. The *Veena* (lute) is now acknowledged to be the most perfect of all musical instruments: It is an Indian <term>instrument</term> <note>Original spelling error: 'instrumnnt'</note> familiar to most of the Hindu gentlemen. The perfection of an instrument depends upon its capacity to reproduce the tunes of other instruments, faithfully and with grace without its own music being inimitable. If this definition is correct then we can undoubtedly classify *Veena* as the most perfect of musical instruments of all nations and of all times. I have seen experts on Veena reproducing most wonderfully the music of other instruments, while none of the hundreds of Indian and European musical instruments can hardly imitate the *Ten Types of Ornamentation* <note>original: "Dasavida Gamakas" — the essential melodic graces or shakes used in Indian classical music</note> which are taught to the student of Veena as elementary portion of his lessons. If therefore Saraswathi must have an instrument by the sounds of which she can encharm the whole worldly phenomena, She has rightly been given Veena to accomplish that purpose. She is the *Inner Apartment* <note>original: "Suddantham" — the sacred or private quarters; here implying the very essence or consort</note> of Brahma, the Creative Power. That part of Him, which appreciates all knowledge and which is conscious of it is termed Saraswathi. Some quote the 2nd stanza which says that the *Astrological Treatise of Parasara* <note>original: "ParasaraHora"</note> is the safest and most reliable in this <term>Kaliyug</term> <note>The current "Age of Iron" or darkness in the Hindu cycle of time</note> and therefore the most valuable for students. But this is omitted by some manuscripts. Parasara was the father of Vaysa one of the greatest names in Aryan Religion and he is the author of various excellent sanscrit works. In the department of law, Parasara stands unrivalled as his *Code of Law* <note>original: "Smrithi" — traditional legal and social texts</note> is considered as of the greatest authority in this <term>yuga</term> <note>An epoch or era of time</note>. It is therefore an admissable fact, that when an author compiles from the works of Parasara he is entitled to the highest authority as that <term>Maharishi</term> <note>A "Great Seer" or enlightened sage</note> seems to have had a peculiar knowledge of the present times and the circumstances which would suit them best.

<summary>The author discusses the mnemonic power of Sanskrit poetry, the symbolic perfection of the Goddess Saraswati's Veena, and establishes the sage Parasara as the ultimate authority for astrology in the current age.</summary>
<keywords>Sanskrit scholarship, Saraswati, Veena, Parasara, Kaliyuga, Hindu law, astrology, musical theory</keywords>

->I shall explain the results of the planetary periods according to the system of the lunar mansions,<-
->following the rules of the Horoscopy of Parasara in the prescribed manner. (2)<-
<note>original: "Phalani nakshatra dasha prakarena vivrinmahe; Vayam Parasharim horamanusritya yathavidhi." This verse establishes the author's methodology, focusing on the Nakshatra Dasha system—a method of timing events based on the moon's position at birth.</note>

## STANZA II.

I shall try to explain the most important principles of astrology in an intelligible manner and I shall be guided in the compilation of my present book by the works of the renowned <term>Maharishi Parasara</term><note>A "Great Seer" (Maha-Rishi) credited with founding the foundational system of Indian astrology.</note>.

## NOTES.

The author Venkatesa Pundit does not pretend to give the readers any original information on astrology. He frankly tells us the source from which he draws his knowledge and he commands respect not for the original matter he puts before his readers, but for the original way in which he puts them before the world. He is very concise and exact to the point he wishes to illustrate. The authority he has quoted is the highest we have in the <term>Aryan</term><note>In this historical context, "Aryan" refers to the ancient Indo-Aryan cultural and linguistic tradition of India.</note> sciences or literature. The family of Parasara has produced the greatest number of <term>Maharishis</term> whom the world has ever seen. In the case of other Maharishis they alone command respect individually by their intellectual greatness while their ancestors or progeny are not known or are not men of equal mental calibre. In the family of Parasara we have a series of really great men.

Vasista, the venerable <term>Maharishi</term>, than whom no <term>Rishi</term><note>A sage or enlightened poet-seer.</note> stands greater in the intellectual or religious world was the founder of this dynasty of literary giants. He was the preceptor and priest of Rama the greatest of Indian heroes and incarnation of Vishnu. His advice to Rama is embodied in the voluminous work on religious instruction entitled <term>Yogavasista</term>, composed by Valmiki, and extending over 36,000 stanzas or 1½ times the real bulk of Valmiki's <term>Ramayana</term> so familiar to our readers. This <term>Yogavasista</term> is an extraordinary work, and the highest religious and moral precepts are related therein with a grace, and simplicity for which we could never find an equal. Vasista has also written works on <term>Dharmasastras</term><note>Treatises on law, duty, and social conduct.</note>, astronomy, astrology, medicine

<summary>Venkatesa Pundit introduces his work as a concise guide based on the teachings of the sage Parasara and provides a historical defense of the intellectual lineage of the sage Vasista.</summary>
<keywords>Jatakachundrika, Parasara, Venkatesa Pundit, Vasista, Maharishi, Yogavasista, Indian astrology, lineage of sages</keywords>

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

and various other branches of knowledge. *Sakti* <note>Shakti</note> is the son of *Vasista* <note>Vashistha, one of the seven great celestial seers</note>, and various works of eminence are attributed to him. *Parasara* <note>Parashara, the traditional father of Vedic astrology</note> is the son of *Sakti* and grandson of *Vasista* and he has distinguished himself in almost every branch of knowledge. *Vyasa* is the son of *Parasara* and is the great grandson of *Vasista*. The eighteen *Puranas* <note>Ancient Hindu texts covering mythology and history</note> extending over many hundred thousands of *Stanzas*, the compilation of the *Vedas* <note>The oldest scriptures of Hinduism</note>, and the *Brahmasutras* <note>Philosophical texts on the nature of reality</note>, the *Bhagavata* and *Bharata* <note>The Mahabharata epic</note> he has composed; the *Bhagavat Gita* therein embodied will speak for themselves. In addition to these, *Vyasa* stands as an authority on astronomy and astrology. *Vyasa Siddantha* is one of the eighteen eminent treatises on Hindu Astronomy and Astrology. *Suka* is the son of *Vyasa* and has very rightly eclipsed the father by the depth of religious thought and the knowledge of *Brahma Vidya* <note>The science of self-realization or ultimate truth</note>. He is called *Sukayogeendra* <note>Suka, the Lord of Yogis</note> and richly deserves that most envious title so hard to secure and so difficult to preserve.

### STANZA III.

By referring to ordinary works the student can learn the details of the *Jataka Bhaga* <note>The section of astrology dealing with individual birth charts</note>, but I am going to expound in my works principles of astrology which would help the intelligent student in his progress in the astrological studies.

### NOTES.

The author very naturally expects a fair amount of elementary knowledge in the student who studies his book, and with this presumption he says he will give such general principles of astrology as would enable the reader to predict much by reading little. This profession of the learned author shows the great value of his work and its usefulness. By elementary knowledge he means the names of the week days, planets,

The *Astrological Self Instructor* and *Primer* I have written will be found to be of immense help to beginners in astrology as all the preliminary information needed for a student is clearly given there.

<note>original Sanskrit: पश्यंतिसप्तमान्सर्वे शनिजीवकुजाः पुनः। विशेषतश्चत्रिदशा त्रिकोणचतुरश्रगान्॥</note>

## STANZA IV.

All the planets aspect <note>The term 'aspect' refers to the 'glance' or influence a planet casts upon another house from its current position.</note> the 7th house. Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars (Sani, Guru and Kuja) have a special vision. Saturn aspects 3rd and 10th houses better, Jupiter 5th and 9th houses, and Mars 4th and 8th houses.

## NOTES.

All planets aspect the 7th house from the place where they chance to be at the time of birth, or question. By the use of the term *all*, Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars are also included, but though those planets have an aspect in the 7th house, their aspect of that house is not as strong as the special aspects we have already named. The aspects of the houses have much significance in the calculations of astrology and the predictions ventured on the strength of such computations. Good aspects have beneficial while bad aspects have malevolent influences. The aspect of a planet is as powerful as its occupation or combination in producing results good or bad.

The variations of power in the planetary aspects must be attributed to the refraction of their rays through the media through which they have to pass before they fall on the earth or the creatures who tenant its surface. These various modifications of the planetary rays have a great significance in the creation, protection and destruction of all phenomena with which they come in contact.

<summary>This page introduces the fundamental rules of planetary 'aspects,' explaining how specific planets like Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars exert influence over different houses in a birth chart. It emphasizes that a planet's 'glance' or aspect is as significant as its physical placement in determining a person's fate.</summary>
<keywords>planetary aspects, seventh house, Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, astrological vision, planetary rays, Jatakachundrika</keywords>

# MOONLIGHT ON ASTROLOGY.
<note>original: "JATAKACHUNDRIKA"</note>

->All planets that lead the trines <note>the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note> are givers of auspicious results.<-
->The lords of the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses are givers of sinful or inauspicious results. ||5||<-

## STANZA V.

Lords of the 5th and 9th houses are always good, while lords of the 3rd, 6th and 11th are always evil.

### NOTES.

The counting of the planets must always be from the birth time or the rising sign <term>lagna: the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>. The Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> owns Leo <note>original: "Simha"</note>, the Moon <note>original: "Chundra"</note> owns Cancer <note>original: "Kataka"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note> owns Gemini <note>original: "Mithuna"</note> and Virgo <note>original: "Kanya"</note>; Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> is the lord of Taurus <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note> and Libra <note>original: "Thula"</note>, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> owns Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> and Scorpio <note>original: "Vrischika"</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is the lord of Pisces <note>original: "Meena"</note> and Sagittarius <note>original: "Dhanas"</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> owns Capricorn <note>original: "Makara"</note> and Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note>. Their owning a house is different from their occupation of a house.

### STANZA VI.

> When Benefics own quadrants they produce evil. When Malefics own quadrants they produce good; the good and evil planets become more and more powerful as they are lords of 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses; 5th and 9th houses; or 3rd, 6th and 11th houses respectively.

### NOTES.

1st, 4th, 7th and 10th houses are <term>quadrants</term><note>also known as Kendras</note>. 1st, 5th and 9th houses are <term>trines</term><note>also known as Trikonas</note>. 3rd, 6th, 10th and 11th houses are <term>houses of increase</term><note>original: "Oopachayams"</note>. The <term>Sun</term><note>Ravi</note>, <term>Saturn</term><note>Sani</note>, <term>Mars</term><note>Kuja</note>, the weak Moon (<term>waning moon</term><note>original: "Kshinachundra"</note>) and badly associated <term>Mercury</term><note>Buda</note> are classified as evil planets. <term>Jupiter</term><note>Guru</note>, <term>Venus</term><note>Sukra</note>, well-associated <term>Mercury</term>, and the <term>full moon</term><note>original: "Poornachundra"</note> are good planets. Lords of the 1st are weaker than the lords of the 4th, lords of the 4th less powerful than those of the 7th, and lords of the 7th are weaker than—

In the case of evil planets <note>Malefics, such as Mars or Saturn</note> they gradually grow stronger by being owners of the later quadrants or kendras <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and get the highest <note>original: "hightest"</note> power when they become lords of the 10th. The reverse holds good with good planets. ->The lords of the 12th and 2nd from the Ascendant give results according to their association with others,<-

## STANZA VII.

The lords of the 2nd and 12th houses give good or bad results according as they are in conjunction with the lords of other houses and the nature of the houses they occupy.

### NOTES

The lords of 2nd and 12th houses have very peculiar <note>original: "peouliar"</note> ways of influencing the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note>. They are not powerful by virtue of their own merits, but are considerably influenced to give good or evil results by the associations they have and the positions they occupy. Here it seems that where the lords of the 2nd and 12th houses have associations of other planets, they give more the influences of the planets who are with them, than their own individual characteristics. They are considerably influenced by the houses they occupy, the planets who are in conjunction with them, and the position of the lords of the houses they are placed at the time.

# By owning the house of loss relative to the house of fortune, the lord of the 8th house does not bestow good.

## Stanza VIII.

> Lord of the 8th house produces evil because he owns the 12th house from the 9th, which is the house of <term>Bhagya</term> <note>Fortune, prosperity, or wealth</note>. If he chances to be the lord of 1st <note>The ascendant or the self</note> also, he becomes good—

---

->**NOTES**<-

The Lord of the 8th house is bad as it happens to become the 12th house from the 9th. The 9th house is the house of peace or happiness and wealth, and the 12th from that is bad <note>In Vedic astrology, the 12th house from any house represents the loss or expenditure of that house's significations</note>. The reason given here is rather abrupt and cannot be carried out continuously in the case of all houses. It may hold good with the 7th, because the 12th from 8th—a bad house—must necessarily be good. And the 12th from the 7th is bad and that is the 6th house signifying debt, disease and enemies. The 12th from 6th is the 5th and it is good, but the 12th from the 5th is 4th and this should be bad by the above reasoning, but it is not so. The 12th from the 4th is 3rd and it is bad, and the 12th from 3rd is 2nd, and the 12th from the 2nd is 1st or birth and these two are bad. The 12th from the birth is the 12th house and it is bad but the 12th from 12th is the 11th and it is bad, the 12th from the 11th is the 10th, and the 12th from the 10th is 9th and these two are good. From the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant; the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> or birth, two houses on either side happen to be bad, as we have the lords of 12th and 11th, 2nd and 3rd, pronounced as bad. I simply show the reader that the reason given for the 8th holds good in many cases and fails in a few. There may be better reasons, but I have not seen any. When the lord of the 8th also is the lord of the 1st he becomes good. This happens only in two cases, in the whole of the zodiac. For Aries and Scorpio the lord is <term>Kuja</term> <note>The planet Mars</note> and he happens to be lord of 1st and 8th houses.

The commentary explores the mathematical logic of "loss" houses and identifies Mars as the unique planet that can rule both the 1st and 8th houses for Aries and Scorpio ascendants.</summary>

When a child is born in Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> and [the planet] becomes good. A child born in Virgo <note>original: "Kanya"</note> has Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> as the lord of the 3rd and 8th houses, and he becomes bad. The reasoning given above is based upon the rule that the 12th house signifies loss or death. Loss of bad is good, and loss of good is bad.

In the 6th verse it was already explained that good planets owning quadrants are productive of evil, while bad planets owning quadrants are productive of good. Planets owning the 8th house produce evil. The details of these good and evil influences will be elaborately explained in the succeeding three verses.

## STANZA IX.

Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> and Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> owning quadrants become very inauspicious. When they occupy the 2nd and 7th houses <note>known as "Maraka" or death-inflicting houses</note> with such ownership, they become powerful in inflicting certain death to the native.

<header>JATAKACHUNDRIKA.</header>

### NOTES.

It has already been pointed out in the previous notes that <term>Guru</term> <note>Jupiter</note> and <term>Sukra</term> <note>Venus</note> are pure benefics <note>naturally auspicious planets</note> and that when they own quadrants (<term>Kendras</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>) they produce evil. If they own <term>Kendras</term> and also occupy the 2nd and 7th houses (<term>Maraka</term> or death houses) they become very powerful in their periods and subperiods (<term>Dasas</term> and <term>Vidasas</term>) in producing death to the <term>native</term>. The possessor of a horoscope is called technically a <term>native</term> and throughout this and other astrological works, the term <term>native</term> must be understood in this sense, unless the contents give it a different meaning. Take an example. To a person born in <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note>, <term>Sukra</term> is the lord of 2nd and 7th houses, and if he is in the 2nd or 7th houses he will inflict death. One born in <term>Mithuna</term> <note>Gemini</note> will have evil from <term>Guru</term>, and if he chances to be in <term>Kataka</term> <note>Cancer</note> or <term>Dhanas</term> <note>Sagittarius</note>, he will surely cause death as he owns the two <term>Kendras</term> 7th and 10th.

---

->Mercury is next, and then the Moon is similarly disposed. The fault of owning the eighth house, however, does not apply to the Sun and the Moon.<- <note>original: "बुधस्तदनुचंद्रोपि भवेत्तदनुतद्विधः। न रंध्रेशवदोषस्तु सूर्याचंद्रमसोर्भवेत्॥१०॥"</note>

### STANZA X.

> <term>Buda</term> <note>Mercury</note> is less malicious than <term>Guru</term> and <term>Sukra</term> and <term>Chundra</term> <note>the Moon</note> is less than <term>Buda</term>. <term>Ravi</term> <note>the Sun</note> and <term>Chundra</term> do not give evil even when they own the 8th house.

### · NOTES,

We have seen the principle that good planets owning quadrants are inauspicious. <term>Buda</term> is not a complete benefic. His goodness or badness depends upon his associations. Therefore he comes next in order after <term>Guru</term> and <term>Sukra</term>. <term>Chundra</term> comes after <term>Buda</term>, for even without evil association he becomes least auspicious when he is new moon. Therefore when well associated <term>Buda</term>, and full moon own quadrants, they produce evil and if they occupy the 2nd and 7th houses, they become powerful to inflict death, but this power is next to <term>Guru</term> and <term>Sukra</term>. Lords of the 8th house are evil, but <term>Ravi</term>

There are two sets of planets—the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> and the Moon, own each only one house, while Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> own each two houses. For Capricorn <note>original: "Makara"</note> the Sun is the lord of the 8th house Leo <note>original: "Simha"</note> and he does not do evil. The lord of the 8th house is bad, but an exception seems to have been made in favour of the Sun and the Moon, and also those two planets Mars and Venus when they happen to own the 1st or birth signs.

Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> does not become good when he owns only the 10th house. He must own the 5th also to become thoroughly good.

Unless Mars also owns in addition to his 10th, the 5th house, he will not produce much good. This double combination in Mars could only happen when a person is born in Cancer <note>original: "Kataka"</note>. Here he becomes lord of 5th and 10th and becomes exceedingly powerful to do good. When a person is born in Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note> Mars owns 3rd and 10th houses and therefore is

### STANZA XII.

Rahu and Kethu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon, often depicted as the head and tail of a celestial dragon.</note> give the same results as those given by the houses they occupy, the planets they are in conjunction with, and the aspects they are subjected to but with greater strength.

#### NOTES.

Rahu and Kethu are once for all dealt with in this stanza by the author and dismissed. They own no houses, and they are called <term>shadowy planets</term> <note>original: "chayagrahams"</note>. I have explained at great length what is meant by Rahu and Kethu in the Astrological Magazine and students would do well to refer to it for more complete information. They give the <term>fruit</term> <note>original: "Phalam," referring to the karmic results or effects</note> of the <term>Zodiac signs</term> <note>original: "Rasis"</note> they occupy, the planets they are associated with, and the aspects they have. Take an example.

### STANZA XIII.

If the lords of the <term>Centers <gloss>Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</gloss></term> are in conjunction with the lords of the <term>Trines <gloss>Thrikonas; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</gloss></term> without being associated with the lords of other houses, they become extremely powerful in producing good results.

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on planetary ownership and house combinations, specifically focusing on the auspicious union of "quadrant" and "trine" lords.</meta>

<page-num>15</page-num>
<header>THE MOONLIGHT OF BIRTHS <note>original: "Jatakachundrika"</note></header>

## NOTES.

This is rather a difficult but very important stanza. The Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> and the Moon <note>original: "Chundra"</note> own each only one house, while the other five planets—Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note>—own each two houses. The conjunction may be in the act of owning the two houses one of which is a quadrant <term>Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, representing the pillars of a life</term>, and the other a trine <term>Thrikonas; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, representing fortune and merit</term>, or it may refer to two different planets each owning one of these houses, and both being in conjunction in any one of the 12 signs of the zodiac. If the verse <note>original: "sloka"</note> means a conjunction of the ownership of these two houses in one planet, the Sun and Moon form exceptions to this principle as they only own one house, and cannot therefore have a combination of the ownerships of Quadrants and Trines referred to above. In the case of the other five planets, Jupiter and Mercury do not, by their peculiar ownership of corner houses as it were, cannot also own Quadrants and Trines together. Therefore there are only 3 planets who have the special privilege of owning Quadrants and Trines together for children born in 6 rising signs <note>original: "lagnas"</note>. They are Taurus <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note>, Cancer <note>original: "Kataka"</note>, Leo <note>original: "Simha"</note>, Libra <note>original: "Thula"</note>, Capricorn <note>original: "Makara"</note>, and Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note>. Mars is the lord of 5th and 10th for Cancer, and lord of 4th and 9th for Leo; Saturn is lord of 4th and 5th for Libra and lord of 9th and 10th for Taurus; Venus is lord of 5th and 10th for Capricorn, and lord of 4th and 9th for Aquarius. This shows that Mars, Venus and Saturn join ownership of Quadrants and Trines for two Zodiac Signs <note>original: "Rasees"</note> each. Looking to the meaning of the succeeding verses as explained by the author, it is clear that the sense is not restricted to this conception of joint ownership of Quadrants and Trines only in one planet. The union of lords of Quadrants and Trines is clearly indicated and the interpretation must be such as would be consonant with the declared general principles of astrology. The planets in conjunction may either own Quadrants and Trines each in himself or may be lords of the different houses which form Quadrants and Trines. Sanskrit is very flexible and the plural noun *Lords* used in the original Sanskrit can be interpreted in either way or both ways. The compiler lays stress upon the conjunction of the lords of the Quadrants and Trines alone.

<summary>This page explains the complex rules regarding the conjunction of planets that rule over Quadrant (Kendra) and Trine (Thrikona) houses, identifying which specific planets can hold dual-lordship for certain rising signs.</summary>

<page-num>16</page-num>
<header>THE MOONLIGHT OF BIRTHS <note>original: "JATAKACHUNDRIKA"</note></header>

If they are associated with the lords of other houses, their value to produce good, will be considerably lessened. If, in such a conjunction, we have the association of the lords of the 3rd, 6th, 8th, 11th and 12th <note>These are generally considered "malefic" or difficult houses in Vedic astrology</note>, the combination produces little good. In the illustration given on page 6 of this book, the combination of the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> and Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note>, lords of the 4th and 5th in the 10th, has been considerably weakened by their association with Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, lord of the 8th and 11th houses. Still the combination is good and it makes the native concerned famous, educated and influential. The productive capacity of planets combining must always depend upon their relative sources of strength and this must never be forgotten by the astrological students who venture into the thorny fields of prediction. If the lords of the <term>Kendras</term> <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, representing the pillars of life</note> and <term>Thrikonas</term> <note>The 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, representing fortune and merit</note> are exalted without evil associations or aspects, it stands to reason to suppose that they will give better results than when they are debilitated.

---

->The leaders of the quadrants and the trines, even if they possess faults themselves,<-
->Become powerful producers of auspicious results simply by their association.<-
->|| 14 ||<-

### **STANZA XIV**

The lords of the Kendras and Thrikonas in conjunction produce good from the simple fact of their union even when they are weak otherwise.

### **NOTES.**

This stanza when read carefully points to a sort of contradiction to what has already been enunciated in the previous verse <note>original: "sloka"</note>. There he says that if the lords of Kendras and Thrikonas are in conjunction without being associated by the lords of other houses they are powerful in doing good. In this stanza he says that the simple fact of their union is quite—

<summary>This page explains how the combination of specific house lords (Kendras and Thrikonas) creates positive astrological effects, even if those planets are otherwise weakened or flawed. It uses a specific example involving the Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter to illustrate how "malefic" house ownership can dilute a positive yoga.</summary>
<keywords>Vedic astrology, Kendra, Thrikona, planetary conjunction, Yoga Karaka, Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, house lords</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary combinations (Yogas) from the previous page, specifically focusing on the relationship between the lords of the angular and trinal houses.</meta>

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

enough to make them give good even when they are otherwise weak. But on a closer examination there is no contradiction whatever. In the first the idea is that Lords of the <term>Kendras</term><note>Angular houses: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope.</note> and <term>Thrikonas</term><note>Trinal houses: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses; these are considered the most auspicious.</note> ought not to be joined with the lords of other houses when producing good and in this <term>sloka</term><note>A Sanskrit verse or stanza.</note> the idea is that their conjunction is productive of good even when they are not powerful in other ways. The illustrations will make the point clear. The conjunction of the lords of <term>Kendras</term> and <term>Thrikonas</term> when they are exalted, in their own houses, in <term>Moolathrikonas</term><note>Specific signs where a planet is particularly strong, second only to its point of exaltation.</note> and in friendly houses or <term>quadrants</term> cannot produce the same results as when the associated planets are debilitated, in enemies' houses, in unfavourable signs. If the conjoined planets occupy 10th house they give results different from what they would do when they are in the 6th. Even when the sources of strength to the planets are weak, i.e., by owning bad houses, by bad <term>vergas</term><note>Sub-divisional charts used to determine the fine-grained strength of a planet.</note> etc., their conjunction is productive of good when they are lords of <term>Kendras</term> and <term>Thrikonas</term>. One <term>sloka</term> refers to associations and the other to sources of strength and weakness. But in either case the various <term>planetary aspects</term><note>The influence planets exert on one another based on their relative positions.</note>, conditions and other important points connected with them could not be safely neglected in making predictions. All that the author wishes to point out is that the conjunction of the lords of <term>Kendras</term> and <term>Thrikonas</term> is good and will produce results according to their various sources of strength.

---

->If the two lords of the 9th and 10th houses reside in each other's signs,<-
->or if either of them resides in the other's house, it becomes a producer of success.<-
<note>original: "निवसेतां व्यत्ययेन तावुभौ धर्मकर्मणोः। एकत्रान्यतरोवापि वसेच्चेद्योगकारको"</note>

### STANZA XV.

If the lords of the 9th and 10th houses join together and occupy the 9th or 10th, or if the lord of the 9th occupies the 9th and the lord of the 10th occupies 10th house they produce good.

<summary>This page explains that the combination of the lords of the 9th and 10th houses creates a powerful positive influence (Yoga), regardless of their individual weaknesses. It clarifies that while planetary strength varies by position, the specific union of these "angular" and "trinal" lords is inherently auspicious.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Jatakachundrika, specifically focusing on the astrological combinations that lead to worldly success and political power.</meta>

<page-num>18</page-num>
<header>JATAKACHUNDRIKA.</header>

### NOTES.

The union of the Lords of <term>Kendras (Angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th)</term> and <term>Thrikonas (Trine houses: 1st, 5th, and 9th)</term> exercises a beneficial influence. There are four angular houses and three trines. The 1st house is an angle as well as a trine, but it is usually counted as an angle. But for <term>Rajayoga (A combination for royal or political success)</term> the author particularly draws the attention of the reader to the importance of the junction of the lords of the 9th and 10th houses. By the principle explained (see page 7 of this book) in stanza VI, the lords of the 9th and 10th are the most powerful, and their combination is naturally looked for as the best for success. Political success must be understood in a liberal sense. All those appointments which are partly or wholly controlled by the government of any country come under this heading, and a poor <term>peon (an office attendant or messenger)</term> has "Royal Success" as well as the mightiest Emperor. The strength of the planets determines the weight to be attached to the appointment.

Several cases are hinted here by the learned Compiler. Lords of the 9th and 10th may join and occupy any house. They may occupy the 9th or 10th houses together. The lord of the 9th may be in the 10th, and the lord of the 10th may be in the 9th. The lord of the 9th may be in the 9th, and the lord of the 10th in the 10th house. As has already been pointed out, the <term>aspects (the way planets "look" at or influence one another from a distance)</term> of planets are as strong as their combinations. If so, the aspect of the lord of the 9th on the lord of the 10th or vice versa also produces "Royal Success." If they are in the same <term>constellation (Nakshatra)</term> although not in the same <term>Rasi (Zodiac sign)</term>, they produce this success. The first <term>padam (quarter or division of a star)</term> of <term>Krithika (the Pleiades)</term> belongs to <term>Mesha (Aries)</term>, while the other 3 quarters belong to <term>Vrishabha (Taurus)</term>. But if the lords of the 9th and 10th are in that star, they may produce good. 

The supposition indicates the combination of two separate planets. But take the sign of Taurus. The lords of the 9th and 10th are one and the same—<term>Sani (Saturn)</term>. He may be in <term>Makara (Capricorn)</term> or <term>Kumbha (Aquarius)</term>, or he may be in any other house. For one born in <term>Vrischika (Scorpio)</term>, the combination of <term>Chundra (the Moon)</term> and <term>Ravi (the Sun)</term> is good. But the Sun and Moon join only in the Zodiac in any house, or they may join in <term>Kataka (Cancer)</term> or <term>Simha (Leo)</term>, but the combination of the two lordships in one planet of angles and trines is not possible in either of their cases.

<summary>This page explains the astrological "Rajayoga" or "Royal Success," specifically focusing on the powerful combination of the lords of the 9th and 10th houses. It clarifies that this success applies to all levels of government service, from messengers to emperors, depending on planetary strength.</summary>
<keywords>Rajayoga, Hindu astrology, 9th house, 10th house, planetary aspects, Saturn, Sun and Moon, government appointments, zodiac signs</keywords>

# If a relationship is formed in any way between the lords of the Trines
# and the powerful lords of the Angles, that combination becomes a producer of Royal Success.
# || 16 ||

## STANZA XVI

->Lords of Angles <term>Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> joining with or lying between lords of Trines <term>Thrikonas; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</term> produce Royal Success <term>Rajayoga; a combination indicating high status or power</term>.<-

## NOTES.

This is a difficult stanza and can be interpreted in various ways. We have already seen that the combination of the Lords of the Angles and Trines is productive of good. The word *Middle* <note>original: "Madhya"</note> used in the verse <note>original: "sloka"</note> confuses the passage a little. It means middle or "with," and the meaning then changes a good deal. Suppose we have Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> as the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note>, then any one of the lords of the Angles—Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, the Moon <note>original: "Chundra"</note>, Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note>—may be between the two planets the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> and Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, who are lords of trines, or they may be with the lords of the Trines. Where a lord of an Angle occupies a place between two lords of trines and has their aspect or aspects <note>In astrology, an "aspect" is the angular relationship between planets that allows them to influence one another</note> from any one of them, there will be success. The lord of an Angle may occupy a place between lords of Trines, but need not have their aspect or need not combine with any one of them. It is possible to have a planet between two lords of trines, owning an Angle and in conjunction with the lords of any other house. Here the position may not be very suitable if the combining planet is evil. If the word *between* is not considered, then I fail to see the use of this stanza as we have already been told of the production of Royal Success by the conjunction of the lords of the Angles and Trines. The conjunction and aspects of unfavourable planets necessarily take away much of the good which may otherwise arise from the position of these planets. The lords of the 5th and 9th houses are referred to, although we know as a matter of fact that the lord of the 5th is weaker than the lord of the 9th in producing good. The same holds good in the case of the lords of the Angles.

<summary>This page interprets Stanza 16 of the Jatakachundrika, explaining how the positioning of planetary "lords" between or in conjunction with one another creates Rajayoga (Royal Success). It specifically explores the nuances of the Sanskrit term 'Madhya' (middle) and how it affects the strength of these astrological combinations.</summary>

<keywords>Jatakachundrika, Rajayoga, Kendras, Thrikonas, planetary aspects, Hindu astrology, Lagna, Mesha, Sanskrit translation</keywords>

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

It looks sensible to interpret this stanza as meaning, that the mere position of a planet owning a <term>Kendra</term> <note>A "Kendra" refers to the four angular houses of a horoscope (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th), which are considered pillars of strength.</note>, between the two lords of the <term>Thrikonas</term> <note>The "Thrikonas" are the auspicious trine houses (1st, 5th, and 9th).</note>, is sufficient to produce <term>Rajayoga</term> <note>A "Rajayoga" is a planetary combination indicating high status, success, or "royal" fortune.</note>, and this meaning is not inconsistent with what is to follow hereafter. Where a planet is in the vicinity of two beneficial planets it is natural to suppose that their influence makes him yield good results. The lord of the 5th also has been given here a prominent place in the production of <term>Rajayoga</term>.

---

Even in the periods of these planets, the auspicious combination usually occurs; if a period of a different planet falls between the periods of two yoga-producing planets, that intervening planet also produces good results during its sub-periods. <note>original Sanskrit: दशास्वपि भवेद्योगः प्रायशोयोगकारिणोः।दशाद्वयीमध्यगता तद्युक्शुभकारिणां॥१७॥</note>

### STANZA XVII

When an evil planet intervenes between the <term>Dasas</term> <note>A "Dasa" is a major planetary period in a person's life.</note> of two <term>Yoga-karaka</term> <note>A "Yoga-karaka" is a planet that creates success or significant positive results.</note> planets, not in union with them he produces good when his subperiods occur in those planetary periods or when his subperiod comes in any other powerful planetary <term>Dasa</term>.

### NOTES.

The periods of all planets contain the subperiods of all other planets. This stanza is capable of a good number of interpretations. Powerful planets are the lords of trines. For <term>Rajayoga</term> we have to depend upon the strength of the conjunction of the lords of <term>Kendras</term> and <term>Thrikonas</term>. There seems to be a peculiar virtue in their mutual conjunction. If the two planetary periods owning <term>Thrikonas</term> are separated by the period of an evil planet i.e. by the lord of the 3rd, 6th, 8th, 11th, or 12th, houses, they seem to give the unfavourable planet strength to produce good in his subperiods. This may occur in 6 cases. The periods of <term>Chundra</term> <note>The Moon</note> and <term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> are separated by <term>Kuja</term> <note>Mars</note>, an evil planet. <term>Kuja</term>'s subperiod in <term>Chundra</term> or <term>Rahu</term> may be good. <term>Chundra</term>'s and <term>Rahu</term>'s subperiods in <term>Kuja</term> may be good. <term>Kuja</term>'s subperiod in any other fa-

It highlights the importance of planetary sub-periods in determining the timing of a person's fortune.</summary>

A favorable planet may be good, or the sub-period of that powerful planet within the major period of Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> may produce favorable results. We have seen in the last few stanzas that the lords of the Quadrants <note>original: "Kendras"; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>, especially those of the Trines <note>original: "Thrikonas"; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note> are favorable. A planetary period, even when the planet is poorly placed by position, aspect, or association, becomes good when it intervenes between two powerful planetary periods. This occurs by virtue of its lord occupying a middle position, as it were, between two well-situated planets. This strength seems to be chiefly attributable to its being hemmed in by the influences of two powerful planets on either side which produce good.

->**Even evil planets, through association with those causing Royal Union,**<-
->**shall bestow the fruits of that union according to their own sub-periods.**<-
->**|| 18 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "योगकारकसंबन्धाः पापिनोऽपिग्रहास्वतः। तत्तत्भुक्त्त्यनुसारेणदिशेयुर्यौगिकंफलं ॥१८॥"</note>

## **Stanza XVIII.**

Evil planets—namely the lords of the 3rd, 6th, 8th, 11th, and 12th houses—when in conjunction with powerful planets that cause Royal Success <note>term: Rajayoga; a planetary combination indicating high status, wealth, or political power</note>, also produce favorable results in their sub-periods according to their own sources of strength at the time.

### **NOTES.**

We have already shown who the powerful planets are. Lords of the Quadrants are powerful when they are associated with the lords of the Trines. When lords of the 3rd, 6th, 8th, 11th, and 12th houses join with powerful planets, what would be the result? The compiler says that powerful planets lose a part of their strength to produce good when they are joined with evil planets, and powerless planets gain some good by joining with powerful planets. This looks very natural. A good man becomes partly bad by evil associations, while a bad man also modifies a part of his conduct by his good associations. Conjunction has much influence and modifies greatly the nature of the planets thus united. Evil planets give good in their sub-periods when they are in conjunction with planets that cause Royal Success.

<summary>This page explains how the influence of "evil" planets (lords of difficult houses) can be improved through association with powerful "Rajayoga" planets. It uses the metaphor of social association to describe how planets modify each other's character and effects during their active periods.</summary>

->If the two dark planets <note>Rahu and Kethu, the lunar nodes</note> should reside in a quadrant or a trine,<-
->they become producers of success through a relationship with the lord of either one.<-

### STANZA XIX.

When Rahu and Kethu <note>original: "Tamograhou" or dark planets</note> occupy Kendras <term>Kendras: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, known as quadrants</term> or Thrikonas <term>Thrikonas: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, known as trines</term> with the lords of *other* houses, they produce good.

### NOTES.

When Rahu and Kethu occupy Kendras or Thrikonas with other planets they give success. Who are those other planets? If Rahu and Kethu occupy Kendras and they are in union with the lords of Thrikonas they become good. If Rahu and Kethu occupy trines and they are in combination with the lords of quadrants they produce good. In this case, the illustration is simply a repetition of the principle already enunciated, only that instead of the combination of the lords of Kendras and Thrikonas we have the combination of Rahu and Kethu in quadrants or trines with planets in those houses. But the stanza above says "in combination with *other* planets." These other planets may refer to the planets of any other houses than those of quadrants or trines. The violation of a well recognised principle in astrology may not have been contemplated by the author, but in this case of Rahu and Kethu, occupying Kendras or Thrikonas, their combination with any other planets may possibly be productive of good results. If the planets in conjunction are owners of unfavourable signs it is reasonable to expect that they will not be productive of much good but if the planets are owners of quadrants or trines they will produce splendid success. It may be that planets with Rahu and Kethu in Kendras or Thrikonas make those shadowy planets strong, and as they give *Results* <note>original: "Phalam"</note> with greater energy, they become good by their occupying good places in the horoscope.

<summary>This page explains how the shadow planets Rahu and Kethu can become "Yoga Karakas" (producers of success) when placed in auspicious houses and connected with specific house lords. It clarifies that these usually malefic points can yield positive results if they are strengthened by their position in quadrants or trines.</summary>

-># When the lords of the Quadrants and Trines are the same, they become Yoga-producers.<-

## **STANZA XX.**

The union of the lordship of the <term>Kendra</term><note>A Quadrant or angular house: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses.</note> and <term>Thrikona</term><note>A Trine house: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses.</note> in one planet produces good. But when the lords of the other Trines join the lords of Quadrants, they will surely produce better results.

## **NOTES.**

We have already seen that where a lord of the Quadrant joins a lord of the Trine, both produce <term>Rajayoga</term><note>A "Royal Union" or combination of planets signifying high status, success, and prosperity.</note>. Such combination may be in one planet or in two planets. There are some houses whose lords become owners of Quadrants and Trines, and in such cases the author says the union produces good. But when the lords of the Quadrants are distinct from the lords of the Trines, and these two join together in any house, the result will be much better than in the case of the union of the two lordships in one and the same planet. <term>Thula</term><note>The zodiac sign Libra.</note> has <term>Sani</term><note>The planet Saturn.</note> as the lord of 4th and 5th, and <term>Vrishabha</term><note>The zodiac sign Taurus.</note> has him again as the lord of 9th and 10th; but in these two cases the ownership of these houses belongs to one and the same planet, and this is already shown as producing favourable results. But for <term>Vrischika</term><note>The zodiac sign Scorpio.</note>, the Moon is the lord of the 9th and the Sun is the lord of the 10th; the combination of these two is productive of better results. The conjunction of two different planets is better for purposes of Rajayoga than the union of the lordship of the two houses in one and the same planet. Different rays happily blended seem to produce better results than the same rays coming from different houses but belonging to the same planet. Grafting produces more racy fruits than simple fruits produced on the same tree.

<summary>This page explains Stanza 20 of the Jatakachundrika, clarifying that while a single planet owning both a Quadrant and a Trine is beneficial, the conjunction of two separate planets owning those houses is even more powerful.</summary>
<keywords>Kendra, Thrikona, Rajayoga, Saturn, Libra, Taurus, Scorpio, Sun, Moon, planetary conjunctions, Vedic astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary combinations (Yogas) from the Jatakachundrika, specifically focusing on the transition from professional success to the determination of longevity.</meta>

<page-num>24.</page-num>
<header>JATAKACHUNDRIKA.</header>

->If the lords of the 9th and 10th houses are also the lords of the 8th and 11th houses,<-
->the person does not attain success through their mere relationship. || 21 ||<-
<note>original: "धर्मकर्माधिनेतारौ रंध्रलाभाधिपौयदि। तयोस्संबंधमात्रेणनयोगंलभतेनरः॥२१॥"</note>

### STANZA XXI.

Where the lords of the 9th and 10th houses are joined by the lords of the 8th and 11th houses success is endangered.

### **NOTES**

It has been already explained that the combination of the lords of the 9th and 10th houses is always productive of splendid <term>Rajayogam</term> <note>A "King-making" combination; a planetary alignment indicating high status, power, and success.</note> or success. Political success may roughly mean <term>Rajayogam</term>, and will be equivalent to service under the ruling authorities. In the life of man success may be achieved in various departments, such as merchants, bankers, lawyers, brokers, barbers, shoemakers, cooks, cultivators, mechanics, engineers, musicians, authors, poets, dramatists, and so on. In the last 6 or 7 stanzas, the author has entirely devoted his explanation to the combinations of the lords of the <term>Kendras</term> <note>The "Angular" or "Cardinal" houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th), which represent the pillars of a person's life.</note> and <term>Thrikonas</term> <note>The "Trine" houses (1st, 5th, and 9th), considered the most auspicious and representative of fortune and merit.</note> and when these are absent, the man may succeed well in any walk of life but not as an official. The conjunction of the lords of 8th and 11th houses with <term>Rajayogam</term> planets, is bad as they produce evil and corrupt the influences of even good planets like the lords of the 9th and 10th houses. The simple combination of the lords of the 9th and 10th without the union of other planets, and without any aspects is the best for <term>Rajayoga</term>. But where they are joined with the lords of other <term>Kendras</term> and <term>Thrikonas</term> they will produce good.

---

The author now comes to the most important part of man’s career <term>viz.</term> <note>Abbreviation for "videlicet," meaning "namely."</note> the length of life which each man enjoys. He tries to determine in what period and subperiod the <term>native</term> <note>The person whose birth chart is being analyzed.</note> will have death. Readers are particularly requested to carefully bear in mind the principles enunciated by the author as nothing can be more significant than the length of lives of men for any body in this world. There can be no painting without a back ground, and there can be no enjoyment of good or bad results without life to continue them.

## The eighth house is the place of life, and the eighth from that eighth is also such.
## The twelfth houses from both of these are called the houses of death. (22)
<note>original: "अष्टमंह्यायुषस्थानमष्टमादष्टमंचतत्। तयोरपिव्ययस्थानंमारकस्थानमुच्यते॥२२॥"</note>

### STANZA XXII.

The 8th house from the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note> and the 8th from that 8th are called houses of life. The 12th house from these two are called houses of death.

### NOTES.

The 8th house from Birth is the house of life as also the 8th house again from this 8th house. There are therefore two houses *viz.* the 3rd and 8th from <term>Lagna</term> which are called the houses of life. The 12th house from the 3rd is the 2nd house from <term>Lagna</term> and the 12th from the 8th house is the 7th from <term>Lagna</term>. These two, 2nd and 7th houses represent death. Take an example. The 8th from

birth which is <term>Thula</term><note>Libra</note>, is <term>Vrishabha</term><note>Taurus</note>, and the 8th from <term>Vrishabha</term> is <term>Dhanas</term><note>Sagittarius</note>; these two <term>Dhanas</term> and <term>Vrishabha</term> are called houses of life. Practically the 3rd and 8th houses from <term>Lagna</term> are houses of life and the 2nd and 7th are houses of death. When the lords of the houses of life are strong, when good planets join those houses or when those houses are aspected by them they produce long life; if not the result will be unfavourable. Life and death therefore have to be primarily determined by the strength or weakness of the planets who own those houses, who are in conjunction with them, who are occupying those houses and who aspect them. ## STANZA XXIII.

The 2nd house is stronger in inflicting death than the 7th; planets who occupy the 2nd are stronger in killing persons than the lords of the 2nd; those who are in conjunction with the lord of the 2nd are still more powerful in causing death.

### NOTES.

Out of the two houses named for causing death <note>the 2nd and 7th houses, known as Maraka houses</note> the 2nd is stronger. Planets who occupy the 2nd are stronger than the planets who own the 2nd, and the planets who are in conjunction with the lord of the 2nd are the most powerful in causing death. Here the meaning will be illustrated by reference to an example. Take the horoscope given under Stanza 22. Libra <note>original: "Thula"</note> is birth <note>the Ascendant</note>. The 2nd house Scorpio is stronger in inflicting death to the native than the 7th Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>. Planets Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> and Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note> who are in the 2nd house Scorpio <note>original: "Vrischika"</note> are stronger than the lord of the 2nd Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> in causing death. But suppose there were a planet or planets with Mars, lord of the 2nd, they would have been stronger in causing death than even Jupiter and Mercury. In the particular horoscope which is about 58 years old, and belonging to a gentleman in high position, the major period <note>term: Dasa - a planetary period in Hindu astrology that governs a specific span of a person's life</note> of Mars only took away his wife by drowning, and caused him severe illness. But now the period of Jupiter is coming and he will inflict death. He is the lord of the 3rd and 6th houses, he joins the lord of the 9th and 12th houses, and he occupies the 2nd house. This causation of death occurs in the periods or sub-periods according to the relative strength of the death-inflicting planets and also upon the strength of the lord of the birth <note>the ruling planet of the Ascendant</note> on whose power greatly depends the question of longe-

### STANZA XXIV.

The close of the periods of the planets who are with the lords of the 2nd and 7th houses causes death; next the periods of the planets who occupy the 2nd and the 7th houses cause death; if death does not happen in the above circumstances it will be caused in the <term>dasas</term> <note>major planetary periods in a person's life</note> of the lords of the 2nd and 7th houses.

### NOTES.

The author wants to explain clearly the periods when death may be expected. Combination for <term>Balarista</term> <note>infant mortality or afflictions in early childhood</note>, short, middle, and long lives are given in other well known works. The several sources of strength and weakness of the planets are also given in them. Having regard for these well known principles of astrology we have to find out when and by whom the native's death is likely to be caused. There are three cases named here. (1) Planets who occupy the 2nd and 7th houses <note>known as Maraka or 'death-inflicting' houses</note> produce death, (2) planets who are lords of the 2nd and 7th houses inflict death, (3) planets who are not owners of 2nd and 7th houses, and who are not occupying those houses but who

### STANZA XXV.

If the periods of the planets occupying or owning death houses do not come in time to cause death then death may happen in the <term>Dasa</term> <note>A planetary period or stage of life governed by a specific planet.</note> of planets who are not <term>Yoga-karakas</term> <note>Planets that produce prosperity or "success-makers."</note> but who are auspicious and combine with the lords of death houses: if death does not happen in these it must be predicted in the periods of the lords of the 3rd and 8th houses.

### NOTES,

As we go on we shall have occasion to mention some combinations which produce <term>Balarista</term> <note>Infant mortality or death before the age of 8.</note> or early death. There are other combinations which produce <term>Madyarista</term> <note>Death occurring between the ages of 8 and 20.</note> or death after the early childhood period of 8 years, and before 20 years. <term>Yogarista</term> <note>Death occurring between 20 and 32 years of age.</note> extends from 20 to 32 years and these must be noted. Then there are conjunctions for <term>Alpayuryoga</term> (short life), <term>Madhayuryoga</term> (middle life), <term>Poornyuryoga</term> (long life), and <term>Aparamithayuryoga</term> (unlimited life). All these combinations are elaborately detailed in my notes to the <term>Sarvartha-Chintamani</term> <note>A classic 13th-century text on Hindu astrology.</note>.

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

*The Gem of Thought* <note>original: "chintamani"</note> and the Astrological Self Instructor. Please refer to them for full information on this most vital question of life. Without reference to *Major Periods* <note>original: "Dasas"</note> or *Sub-periods* <note>original: "Bhookties"</note> these combinations give to or take away life from the person. We cannot be therefore too careful about these special conjunctions of planets. Planets in death houses produce death; if they do not, then those who own them produce death; if they do not, then planets who do not combine in themselves or with others the lordships of the *Quadrants* <note>original: "Kendras"</note> and *Trines* <note>original: "Thrikonas"</note>, but who are auspicious or good, may cause death; if these planets also do not cause death, then the author wants us to fix the period of death in the *Major Period* or *Sub-period* of the lords of the 3rd and 8th houses. Longevity is first to be determined with reference to *Misfortunes* <note>original: "Aristas"</note> or evil combinations; if they allow the native to live, then by a process called *Life-span granted by the Planets* <note>original: "Graha-Datta Pindayurdaya"</note> it must be determined what each planet, with reference to its own sources of strength and weakness, contributes in the shape of life to the native born, so that the length of life will be the sum total of these various periods of life granted by the planets to the fetus in the womb. This process must be used only after the *Infant Mortality* <note>original: "Balarista"</note> period has expired and not before. We have seen that the lords of the 3rd and 8th houses are evil and they produce death, if others fail. In the next stanza the author determines death in the period of the most evil planet. If all these fail Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> will cause death as he is evil and always inclined to do bad.

->When the death-inflicting planets are not seen,<-
->the wise should imagine death in the periods of purely malefic planets. (23)<-
<note>original Sanskrit: केवलानांचपापानां दशासुनिधनं क्वचित्। कल्पनीयं बुधैर्नृणांमारकानामदर्शने॥२३॥</note>

### Stanza XXVI.

When the periods of death-inflicting planets are not appearing in time to cause death, the planet most inauspicious in the horoscope will cause death at any time.

## NOTES.

As I have already stated there may be some combinations which show that death must happen at a particular period, and that period might not be in time to kill the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read.</note>. Here it is clear that the author wants the student to find out the length of life by *Planetary Contribution to Longevity* <note>original: "Graha Datta Pinda Ayurdaya"; a specific mathematical method in Vedic astrology for calculating a person's lifespan based on planetary positions.</note> and then see which period is likely to correspond to the term of life so found out by mathematical calculations. There are also various combinations mentioned in other works which roughly determine the length of life. Take an example. A child has *Infant Mortality* <note>original: "Balarista"; astrological combinations indicating death in early childhood.</note> which means that it will or may die within 8 years. But 8 years is a long period. We have to find out which period, month, and day will terminate its existence during those 8 years. This must be found out with reference to the periods or subperiods which come in that time. Suppose at the time of birth a <term>Yogakaraka</term> <note>A "planet of success" that rules both a favorable angle and a favorable trine house, bringing prosperity.</note> planet rules the native, he will be unwilling to kill the native himself and his friends also share the same feeling. But if there is strong <term>Infant Mortality</term> <insert>Balarista</insert> combination the child must die, and we have to look to a certain planet as the terminator of the child's existence. Those who are with or aspected by the <term>Yogakaraka</term> do not kill. Then we have to find a planet who is the most inauspicious among the remaining lot and ascribe death to his period or subperiod. In the determination of the extent of evil, we have to look to other works on astrology for information. Planets have debilitations, exaltations, retrogrades, accelerations, humilities, combustions, favourable or unfavourable conjunctions, lateral, periodical and positional influences, ownership of good and bad houses, malefic and benefic aspects, unfriendly influences, and various other sources of strength and weakness which the planets possess at the time of birth. A knowledge of all these becomes absolutely necessary to correctly determine which planet becomes the strongest and which the weakest. Weakest planets are always productive of evil, although they may have been classed as benefics. A debilitated <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: "Guru"</note>, owning <term>angular houses</term> <note>original: "kendras"; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope.</note> or 8th and 11th houses is as mischievous as <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: "Sani"</note> or any other evil planet. ### STANZA XXVIII.

Planets do not give in their Periods and sub-periods all the good or evil they are able to do by their positions, associations and aspects in the horoscope.

### NOTES.

A planet is influenced to do evil or good for various reasons. His occupation, his owning, association, exaltation, debilitation, retrogrades, accelerations and aspects, his enemies and friendships have much to do with the results which he produces upon the native. Every planetary period begins with its own sub-period and its further minor periods until we reach the minutest time possible. Several systems of calculations of these planetary periods are recommended in the Astrological works. # JATAKACHUNDRIKA:

In this work on <term>Astrology</term> <note>specifically Jyotisha, the traditional Hindu system of astronomy and astrology</note>, the period of the most powerful planet in the <term>Kendras</term> <note>the four angular houses of a horoscope: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</note> is the first in order. There are the <term>Vimsottari</term> (120 years) and <term>Astottari</term> (108 years) systems prevalent in Southern and Northern India respectively. The terms of years ascribed to the planets also differ. In Southern India, <term>Oodudasa</term> <note>also known as Udu Dasa; a system where planetary periods are based on the Moon's position in a specific lunar mansion</note> or periods calculated according to the rising constellation at the time of birth are in vogue, and they are easy to determine. In another system, the most powerful planet, including the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note>, begins as the lord of the first period. The strength of the planets has to be found by the sources of power prescribed in the Astrological works elsewhere—such as:

*   <term>Sthanabala</term> <note>Positional strength</note>
*   <term>Digbala</term> <note>Directional strength</note>
*   <term>Kalabala</term> <note>Temporal strength</note>
*   <term>Viryabala</term> <note>Strength of valor or essence</note>
*   <term>Chestabala</term> <note>Motional strength</note>
*   <term>Nysargikabala</term> <note>Natural or inherent strength</note>
*   and the various good and evil <term>Vergas</term> <note>Harmonic divisional charts</note>.

The reader is referred to my English translation of the *Sarwartha Chintamani* <note>a classic 13th-century Sanskrit text on predictive astrology</note> in the first Chapter for full details on this important branch of knowledge. Find out the <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>Lunar mansion or star constellation</note> at the time of birth and see how much of it passed and how much remains. Take the Sun's period, which is 6 years. This represents the Sun's general period. Then we have <term>Vidasa</term> or sub-period; 3rd we have the <term>Bhookti</term> or sub-sub-period; 4th <term>Antardasa</term> or sub-sub-sub-period; 5th we have <term>Antarantara</term> or still smaller division. Then we have the <term>Sookshma</term> or still smaller division and <term>Prana</term> or the smallest of the planetary sub-divisions, enabling us to make hourly or even half-hourly predictions.

Take <term>Ravi Dasa</term> <note>The major period of the Sun</note> of 6 years. The planets have an invariable proportion in the distribution of their periods and sub-periods. It is seen that the Sun gets 6 years out of 120 years (the total of the planetary periods). In the period of 6 years, his sub-period bears the same proportion as his general period bears in the 120 years. When he has 6 years for 120 years, what will he have in 6 years?

6 × 6 / 120 years = 36 / 120 years.

As the denominator is greater, we have to reduce the fraction to months—thus we have:
36 × 12 / 120 = 36 / 10 = 18 / 5 = 3 3/5 months.
And we have 3/5 × 30 days = 18 days.

The sub-period of <term>Ravi</term> <note>The Sun</note> in <term>Ravi's</term> general period is 3 months and 18 days. In all the other minuter divisions, <term>Ravi</term> bears the same constant proportion of 6 to 120. If we take <term>Ravi's</term> sub-sub-period, we have:
3 3/5 months = 18 / 5 × 6 / 120 months

->= 18 / 5 × 1 / 20 = 18 / 100 = 9 / 50 months =<-
->9 / 50 × 30 days = 27 / 5 = 5 2/5 days.<-

Now if we require further sub-divisions we have to follow the same
