### **STANZA XXIX.**

Planets who are with the lords of the periods, planets who occupy the houses of the lords of the periods and planets who are otherwise equal in strength to the lords of the periods, give similar results as the lords of the periods themselves, in their periods and sub-periods.

### **NOTES.**

The Period <term>Dasa; the major planetary cycle in a person's life</term> and its Sub-Divisions have to be calculated with reference to the rising constellation at the time of birth. That is called the *Birth Star* <note>original: "Janma Nakshatra"</note>. I have already given the Periods (Periods of planets) for the 27 constellations. The constellation *Krittika* <note>The Pleiades</note> at birth commences with the period of the Sun. In judging of the planetary influences, in their periods, sub-periods and all the minor divisions, the reader has to bear in mind the several sources of strength and weakness which the planets have. If one planet occupies the house of another planet and the latter is the lord of the period <term>Dasanatha; the planetary "ruler" of a specific life stage</term> then the former gives results similar to those given by the *Lord of the Period*. Those who are also in conjunction with the *Lord of the Period*, produce similar results to him. The owner of the house, occupied by the *Lord of the Period* produces results similar to the latter. Other planets which are similar in strength or weakness as the *Lord of the Period* give the same results. Planets are said to be similar in strength when they occupy houses of the same nature, or possess lateral, periodical, positional and other advantages detailed in the Astrological works and to which constant references have already been made. This is a direction given to the Astrological student to measure the strength of the planets in producing results, when their sub-periods and other minute divisions intervene in those of the general periods. All planets send out light to us though it may be the reflected solar ray. The effects of a solar ray pure and simple must be different from the effects of the same ray when reflected

## STANZA XXX.

In the periods and sub-periods of other planets, unconnected with the lord of the general <term>Dasa</term> <note>The major planetary period currently ruling a person's life</note> the results are to be predicted otherwise when they are opposed to him and inclined to give contrary effects.

## NOTES.

In stanza 30 the author stated that in the general period of a planet we have to expect similar results to *him* if we get the sub-periods or other minor divisions of planets who are with him, or in his house, or who own the house <term>Dasanatha</term> <note>The lord or ruling planet of the major period</note> occupies. There must necessarily be two or three sets of planets in the horoscope of every man. One set are directly connected with the <term>Dasanatha</term> or are otherwise under his special influence by conjunction, aspect or occupation. There are then some other planets who are directly opposed to the influences of the lord of the general <term>Dasa</term> and who try always to give their own or who try to modify the results of the <term>Dasanatha</term>. There will be a third set who are partly that way and partly this way, a sort of indifferent party. The author tries to explain the results of sub-periods belonging to the planets who are opposing the lord of the <term>Dasa</term>. When the lord of the <term>Dasa</term> is for giving good health and the lord of the <term>Bhookti</term> <note>A sub-period within the major Dasa</note> (sub-period) is for producing sickness, what would be the result? The *native* <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note> becomes indifferent in health. If <term>Dasanatha</term> is for giving gold and money and the <term>Bhooktinatha</term> <note>The lord or ruling planet of the sub-period</note> is for stealing it away, the person will be possessed of gold and money and loses them or will have them in small quantities. ## STANZA XXXI.

In the *<term>Dasa</term>* <note>The major planetary period in a person's life.</note> of the lord of 5th or 9th houses, who is not otherwise powerless, the lord of the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, though unconnected with the *<term>Dasanatha</term>* <note>The "Lord of the Period" or the planet ruling the current major cycle.</note>, produces good. Similarly *<term>Bhooktinatha</term>* <note>The "Lord of the Sub-period" who rules a smaller division of time within the major Dasa.</note> who chances to be owner of one or more quadrants and who is not otherwise weakened produces good in the *<term>Dasa</term>* of the lord who governs 5th and 9th houses.

### NOTES.

This stanza is capable of several interpretations. The *<term>Dasa</term>* of the lords of the 5th and 9th has already been declared to be good. But the lord of the 5th or 9th may also be the lord of 6th, 8th, 12th or other inauspicious signs, may be in conjunction with evil planets may have malicious aspects, and may be debilitated <note>A planet is debilitated when it is in a zodiac sign where its influence is at its weakest.</note>. Here deductions must be made from the general powers of the lords of 5th and 9th houses for doing good. Suppose we have the sub-period of a lord of the quadrants in such a *<term>Dasa</term>* what will be the result? The author says that if the lord of the sub-period owns any of the quadrants (*<term>Kendras</term>* <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, considered the pillars of the horoscope.</note>) and has no evil influences detailed above in the case of the ruler of the 5th and 9th houses and has no connection with the *<term>Dasanatha</term>* he produces good in his sub-period. The author apparently seems to make no provision in a reverse case.

Take an example. We have the *<term>Dasa</term>* of a planet who owns any of the quadrants, and who is otherwise powerful. Does he give us good

All planets—good or evil—who own 5th or 9th houses produce good, while all planets who own quadrants are not declared as productive of auspicious results. It seems clear that the value of the planets who rule 5th or 9th house in producing good is greater than the value of the planets who govern *Quadrants* <note>original: "Kendras"</note>—1-4-7-10. Benefics ruling quadrants are producers of evil while malefics governing *Quadrants* are productive of favourable results. Whether good or evil planets, they produce good when they own the 5th and 9th houses.

### STANZA XXXII.

Success <note>original: "Rajayoga"; literally 'King-union,' a combination indicating high status or power</note> comes in the major periods of death-inflicting planets. Evil planets in those sub-periods <note>original: "Bhooktis"</note> not only work against this success but also bring death to the *native* <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note>.

### NOTES.

Lords of the 2nd and 7th houses are death-giving planets and these two houses are called *Death-inflicting* <note>original: "Maraka"</note> houses.

The lords of these houses give success in their *major periods*. But when the *sub-periods* of evil planets intervene, the success predicted will be negatived and they bring death also. Planets who are in conjunction with death-inflicting lords give prosperity. Those who are not with them but otherwise favourably disposed will give limited fortune, and those who are not in conjunction with them and who are maliciously disposed give ruin and death. The words *Evil planets* have two significations. Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note>, the Full Moon <note>original: "Poornachundra"</note> and well-associated Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note> are generally termed good planets or *Benefics*. While Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note>, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, the New Moon <note>original: "Kshinachundra"</note> and badly associated Mercury are called evil planets or *malefics*. On a closer study and greater familiarity with the subject the student will at once see that each of the two words *evil* and *bad* when used with benefics <note>planets that are naturally favorable, like Jupiter or Venus</note> and malefics <note>planets that are naturally harsh, like Saturn or Mars</note> or *vice versa* has a limited meaning and gives no room for confusion. Good planets in conjunction with the lords of the death-inflicting houses <note>known as Marakas</note> give success. ### STANZA XXXIV.

Planets which are disposed to give *Royal Success* <note>original: "Rajayoga"</note> will do so in their sub-periods, which *intervene* during the general periods of planets with which they are not associated.

---

### NOTES.

The stanza is plain enough. A planet which becomes good for several reasons wants to produce success to the <term>native</term> <note>The person whose birth chart is being analyzed.</note>. His own <term>Major Period</term> <note>original: "Dasa"</note> may not come at all during the life of the person. But the sub-periods of planets being much shorter in duration may come and go away many times during his life. It has been remarked above that planets in combination with those who are good will also give success. This may be due to the purification of his own rays by conjunction with the purer rays of the nobler planet, or by the rays of the weaker planet becoming inactive on account of the superior power of the rays of the more exalted one. In either way the result is auspicious. But if there is no conjunction between the lords of the general and the sub-periods, then the results will be good or bad according to the nature of the individual planets whose power is exercised. The sub-period of a well situated planet will give success in the general period of a planet with which he is not even united.

---

success in their respective sub-periods according to their relative strength.

### NOTES.

<term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon, often called the Dragon’s Head</note> and <term>Kethu</term> <note>The South Node of the Moon, often called the Dragon’s Tail</note> have no independent mansions which they own. They are called shadowy planets or <term>shadow</term> (<term>chaya</term>) <term>planets</term> (<term>Grahams</term>) or <term>dark</term> (<term>Thamo</term>) <term>planets</term> (<term>Grahas</term>). But some of the later writers have given these planets places of exaltation and debilitation. Exaltation means, for good or evil planets, the state of a planet in a particular house where it is capable of producing the greatest possible good. Debilitation means the opposite state. These two states of exaltation and debilitation are called <term>High</term> (<term>Oocha</term>) and <term>Low</term> (<term>Neecha</term>) respectively in Sanskrit. In the latter state, the planet will be inclined to do the greatest possible harm to the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note>. When a planet is in full glory, the rays seem to have a peculiar effect in producing much success and happiness. When the planet is eclipsed or its rays are greatly disturbed and modified for worse, they become useless and produce misery and failures. From <term>Aries</term> (<term>Mesha</term>) to <term>Virgo</term> (<term>Kanya</term>), <term>Rahu</term> is considered powerful and he tries to do good. From <term>Libra</term> (<term>Thula</term>) to <term>Pisces</term> (<term>Meena</term>), <term>Kethu</term> is good and gives success. <term>Taurus</term> (<term>Vrishabha</term>) is specially strong for <term>Rahu</term> while <term>Scorpio</term> (<term>Vrischika</term>) is so considered for <term>Kethu</term>. There are some differences in ascribing houses of exaltation and debilitations and I shall represent the most approved doctrine here without entering into unnecessary details.

->If the lords of the major periods are evil, the sub-periods of favorable planets<-
->unconnected to them will yield evil results.<-
->The sub-periods of favorable planets that are connected to them<-
->will yield mixed results. The sub-periods of those planets that cause<-
->great success will yield extremely evil results if they are<-
->unconnected to the evil major lord.<-

# JATAKACHUNDRILA.

Planets who are capable of giving *auspicious combinations* <note>original: "Yogam"; refers to specific planetary alignments that bring prosperity</note> and who are not connected with the lords of the *major periods* will give good results in their sub-periods during such *major periods*.

->NOTES.<-

Again the idea seems to be repeated. It may be to make the matter more emphatic that the author is laying much stress on this point. There is however a nice distinction drawn by the author. He seems to make a difference between good planets *benefics* <note>original: "Shubhagrahas"</note> and planets which are said to be powerful or *indicators of success* <note>original: "yoga karakas"</note>. The meaning of the latter expression seems to be that those planets which are not good in nature, but become powerful by association or otherwise are called *indicators of success*. *Jupiter* <note>original: "Guru"</note>, *Venus* <note>original: "Sukra"</note>, *the Full Moon* <note>original: "Poornachundra"</note>, and well associated *Mercury* <note>original: "Buda"</note> are called *benefic planets*. But when they are debilitated, badly associated, or occupying evil houses they produce evil and may be called evil planets. But there is the general term *evil* applicable to *Mars* <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, *the Sun* <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, *Saturn* <note>original: "Sani"</note>, badly associated *Mercury* and *the Waning Moon* <note>original: "Kshinachundra"</note> along with the *North Node* <note>original: "Rahu" - implied by context</note> and *South Node* <note>original: "Kethu"</note>. These also may produce good or bad according as they own good or evil houses, combine with good or evil planets, and have exaltations or debilitations. Therefore *benefic planets* are different from *planets that indicate success*.

The distinction deserves to be specially remembered as the *results* <note>original: "Phalam"</note> to be predicted depends largely upon these considerations. *Saturn* for instance can own 9th and 10th houses and then becomes very powerful, and if also exalted and well associated he will produce splendid results. So also any other evil planet. Under these circumstances he will be called a *success-indicating* planet and not a *benefic*. If *Jupiter* owns 8th and 11th houses he becomes evil and produces bad. The author seems to have three different cases under contemplation in the treatment of *major periods* and *sub-periods* <note>original: "Dasas and Bhookties"</note>. (1). Where the lords of the periods are evil and the lords of the sub-periods are favourable and not in conjunction with them the result will be unfavourable. (2). Where they join the lords of the Periods they produce indifferent results. (3). Where the lords of the sub-

The conjunction of even evil planets seems to mitigate the bad they would otherwise inflict on the *subject of the horoscope* <note>original: "native"</note>. A little repetition I am afraid occurs here. The idea is already explained in the previous verse. Planets unconnected with the lord of the *Major Period* <note>original: "Dasa"</note> will give good if they are otherwise powerful, during their sub-periods. Here the author seems to particularise the time when those planets who are not in conjunction with the lord of the general *Major Period* will produce good. The time indicated is the sub-period <note>original: "Bhookti"</note> which intervenes during the *Major Period* of the planet with whom the lord of the *Sub-period* is not associated. It must be remembered that this is a small work in which at most, only the barest outlines of astrology could be given. All the details of the complicated Astrological science are not possible to be clearly enumerated in a work which is only elementary in its nature and therefore the readers must have resort to other comprehensive treaties to make certain prediction. Predictions are the fruits of the Astrological sciences and they are therefore the hardest to produce. Primers treating of Botany, Physics Physiology, Chemistry, Geology, Biology or Psychology cannot pretend to be exhaustive and are generally more intended to rouse the curiosity of the students for making deeper studies in those branches knowledge than to be the sure guides on which the greatest confidence could at once be placed. From the very elementary nature of the books, they must be considerably defective and generally superficial.

the sub-periods of *malefics* <note>original: "malifics"; refers to planets that naturally cause harm or difficulty</note> who are not in conjunction with him.

## NOTES.

There are two combinations contemplated here and they must be specially borne in mind. First we have to determine who is the most painful planet to inflict death <note>The "Maraka" or death-inflicting planet</note> and then if his general period is long we have next to determine in whose *sub-period* <gloss>Bhookti</gloss> death is caused. Life is the most important question for any person and therefore his longevity must first be considered and determined. Then the good and bad results in his life have to be predicted. Prospects in life have no value whatever unless we know the length of the span of our existence. The author says that in the *major period* <gloss>Dasa</gloss> of the *death-inflicting* <gloss>Maraka</gloss> planet, the *sub-period* <gloss>Bhookti</gloss> of the favourable planet who is in conjunction with him does not produce death, but the *sub-period* <gloss>Bhookti</gloss> of the evil planet who is not in conjunction with the *lord of the major period* <gloss>Dasanatha</gloss> will produce certain death. The question may then be raised as to whether the *sub-period* <gloss>Bhookti</gloss> of the evil planet who is in conjunction with the *death-inflicting major period lord* <gloss>Maraka Dasanatha</gloss> produces death or favourable results? From a careful perusal of the above verses it appears clear that the periods and sub-periods of even the worst planets when they are in conjunction with each other do not inflict such evil as the periods and sub-period of planets who are not in conjunction with each other. Therefore there is some good in the very act of conjunction or combination which should not be lost sight of by the student of Astrology when he attempts to make his predictions. I have already stated the results of the sub-periods of the favourable planets when they are not in conjunction with the planets whose periods govern the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note> for the time being.

->Will bestow, in an inverse manner, specifically good and bad results.<-

### Stanza XXXIX.

*Venus* and *Saturn* (*Sukra* and *Sani*) give good or bad results in their respective periods and sub-periods inversely. <note>This refers to a famous astrological exception where these two planets often yield their most significant results in each other's sub-periods rather than their own.</note>

## NOTES.

These two planets <note>Saturn and Venus</note> seem to have a peculiar affinity and produce good or evil in an intense form inversely in *their* *Major Periods* <term>Dasas: the primary planetary cycles in a person's life</term> and *Sub-periods* <term>Bhookties: the smaller cycles within a Major Period</term>. If *Saturn* <note>Sani</note> is good and his *Major Period* governs man, the *Sub-period* of *Venus* <note>Sukra</note>, when he is well disposed, produces much excellent results. The author says that even if there are other Sub-periods equally favourable like that of *Venus* they will not produce such splendid results as *Venus* can do. Thus in the *Major Periods* of *Saturn* and *Venus* and their sub-periods intervening them, the greatest fortune may be expected if they are favourably disposed. Whatever may be the occupation of the planets or their association, *Saturn* and *Venus* will produce the most intense effects during their periods and sub-periods, which no other two planets could produce. The author has taken us far into the fields of Astrology and has shown by brief but very expressive stanzas the results of many principles guiding the life of the creature. He has shown the relative merits of the planets, the periods of successes and failures, the principles on an examination of which death may safely be predicted and a general survey of the other items which give strength to the planets. These are all general results and now in the 40th stanza and the subsequent ones, he is going to make a summary of the results, which the native born in each of the 12 Zodiacal signs, is likely to enjoy. This will help the student at once to see which of the planets are favourable and which are not in judging of the different *ascendants* <term>lagnas: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> in which people are born.

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

### NOTES.

In the first thirty-nine stanzas of his work, the author has tried to explain briefly the general principles of Astrology, and they will be now summarized, as it were, for the convenience of the readers. There are 12 Zodiacal signs, and he will give the principal good and bad results which accrue from the different planetary combinations in each of these 12 houses. Which planets are good in <term>Mesha</term> (Aries), which are bad, and whose combinations produce <term>Raja Yoga</term> (combinations for high status or political success), whose conjunction inflicts misery, and who are likely to produce death and in whose period and sub-period—these and similar points are determined with reference to each of the houses and each of the planets; and therefore we request the astrological students to carefully follow the author in the important stanzas which are given for their guidance. Of the several works which the translator had read on Astrology, he has much confidence in the working out of the general principles laid down here, as they will be amply verified at every step of the reader in the line of his prediction.

->Saturn, Mercury, and Venus are malefic; Jupiter and the Sun are auspicious.<-
->The mere combination of Saturn and Jupiter does not produce favorable results. || 41 ||<-
<note>original: "मन्दसौम्य सिताः पापाः शुभौ गुरु दिवाकरौ । नशुभं योगमात्रेण प्रभवेच्छनिजीवयोः ॥ ४१ ॥"</note>

### STANZA XLI Mesha—Aries.

A Person born in ***Mesha Lagna*** (Aries as the rising sign at birth) has ***Sani*** (Saturn), ***Buda*** (Mercury), and ***Sukra*** (Venus) ill-disposed towards him. ***Guru*** (Jupiter) and ***Ravi*** (the Sun) are auspicious. The simple combination of ***Guru*** and ***Sani*** produces no beneficial results.

### NOTES.

Here the author has drawn the attention of the readers to 3 most important points to be consulted in estimating the good and evil results which are likely to happen to the person born while <term>Mesha</term> (Aries) is rising at the time of birth. <term>Sukra</term> (Venus) is the lord of the 2nd and 7th houses, <term>Buda</term> (Mercury) is the lord of the 3rd and 6th houses, and <term>Sani</term> (Saturn) is the Lord of the 10th and 11th houses. Of course, it has already been pointed out in the earlier portions of this work, that the Lords of the

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA.

The 3rd, 6th, 8th, 11th, and 12th houses are considered unfavorable. Therefore, Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note>, being the Lord of the 3rd and 6th houses for a person born in Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>, becomes an evil influence. Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> owns the 2nd and 7th houses. Benefic planets <term>Benefic: planets that naturally bring positive results, like Jupiter or Venus</term> owning quadrants <term>Quadrants: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, also called Kendra</term> are considered to lose their positive power and become "evil" in this context, and the 7th is one of these quadrants. Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> owns the 10th and 11th houses; of these, the 11th is bad, while the 10th house (a quadrant) owned by a naturally malefic planet like Saturn is considered good. Mercury is entirely unfavorable, but in the cases of Venus and Saturn, one of the two houses each planet owns is evil and the other is good. However, Mercury, Saturn, and Venus are not friends with the Lord of Aries—namely, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>. This point is fully explained in the *Astrological Self Instructor and Primer* published by me in English. 

Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> and the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> are declared to be good. The Sun owns the 5th house from Aries, and Jupiter owns the 9th and 12th houses. It has been already shown that the Lords of the trines <term>Trines: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, forming an equilateral triangle in the chart</term> are productive of good results. The Sun owns only one house and is perfectly good, but Jupiter owns the 9th as well as the 12th, of which the 9th is good and the 12th is bad. Jupiter, the Sun, and Mars (Lord of Aries) are friends; thus, though Jupiter owns one of the evil houses, he will, in the main, produce good and not evil. 

The third point the author refers to is the non-production of the <term>Raja Yoga: a planetary combination indicating high status, power, or "royal" success</term> by the combinations of the Lords of the 9th and 10th houses. This sounds contrary to his own declared principles enunciated in Stanza 16 of this work, where he says that Raja Yoga is produced by the combination of the Lords of the quadrants and trines. But in the case of these two planets <note>Jupiter and Saturn</note>, they not only own the 9th and 10th houses—whose combination ought to produce good—but they are also Lords of the 12th and 11th houses, whose combination acts against the production of the Raja Yoga (see page 24). Therefore, the author says clearly that for a person born in Aries, the simple conjunction of Saturn and Jupiter alone is not sufficient to produce political success. We must look to other combinations for Raja Yoga. The line of argument I adopt is simple enough, and the reader is earnestly requested to note its force in making predictions when consulting horoscopes.

### **STANZA 42.**

But on the other hand, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> when combined with Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note>, not only does not provide good results but will actively produce evil. Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> does not himself kill the subject <note>original: "native"</note> although he possesses death-inflicting powers <note>term: Maraka (death-inflicting powers)</note>.

### **NOTES.**

It was pointed out (see stanza 41) that Jupiter, being the lord of the 9th and 12th houses, has both good and bad qualities combined within himself.

In the combination of Saturn and Jupiter, the author asked us not to expect any good results, and he here tells us that there will be evil in their combination, especially on the part of Jupiter. This requires a clear explanation. Let us take an example. We know that a planet has good and evil influences due to various causes explained in the astrological works. If a planet is capable of giving one unit <note>original: "rupee worth"</note> of good and also one unit of evil, the result will be that the planetary influence is neutral—good cancelling the evil. However, the astrological writers assure us that the result is not only not neutral, but is bad. They are of the opinion that when good and evil influences of a planet are equal, we must predict evil, but in a milder form. Remembering then this principle of their explanation, we shall see why Jupiter is bad in the combination with Saturn. Jupiter owns the 9th and 12th houses, of which the 12th for Jupiter and 11th for Saturn are considered unfavorable houses. Here the evil is equal to the good these two planets have, as one of the two houses each planet owns is good while the other is bad. We can understand that the combination is bad, but why should Jupiter be particularly declared bad in producing evil in the combination, while Saturn also contributes to bring about the combined evil result? Jupiter is an auspicious planet by nature and evil influences seem to affect him more; Saturn is an inherently "malefic" or evil planet and good influences seem to make him better. Jupiter owns one good and one bad house, and they neutralize each other's effects, but he has an evil association, namely, of being in conjunction with Saturn. There is therefore something more than good and bad counteracting each other, and Jupiter is declared to produce evil because he has an evil combination. But Saturn has a better prospect. His owning good and...

Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> owns the 2nd and the 7th houses and these two are called death-inflicting houses <note>The 2nd and 7th houses are known as Maraka houses in Vedic astrology, associated with the end of life</note>. If a planet owns one death-inflicting house it is enough to predict evil results, but when he owns two death-inflicting houses, we should naturally expect to see that he will kill the person undoubtedly. But though Venus has jurisdiction to kill by governing the 2 houses, 2nd and 7th, he will not kill the <term>native</term> <note>The person whose birth chart is being analyzed</note> himself but will hand over the person to others who are also ill-disposed towards the person born in Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> and who would do the execution work.

### STANZA XLIII.

Evil planets like Saturn &c., will cause death to the person; try and find out the results good and bad which might happen to the person born in Aries carefully and with reference to the principles of astrology.

### NOTES.

Just in the previous stanza we pointed out that when Jupiter and Saturn combine together Jupiter will inflict evil. Now we have to determine who will kill the <term>native</term>. The author says “evil planets like Saturn &c.” He takes Saturn first and includes Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note> and occasionally the Moon <note>original: "Chundra"</note>. Mercury is bad, as he owns third and sixth houses. But the Moon is the lord of 4th. If he is full then, he will be classified as good and his owning one of the <term>quadrants</term> <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are considered pillars of the horoscope</note> is bad and he may, by evil combinations, produce death. But if he is weak, then his owning the quadrant is auspicious and he will do good. These then are the planets who determine the death of the person born while the sign <term>Aries</term> <note>original: "Mesha"</note> is rising at the time of birth. I have given notes at considerable length, but the fuller the explanations offered even at the risk of repetition, the better it is for beginners who read these

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA

works and who, I am sure, will be very much profited by the extra information given by me herein.

The author has already shown us very briefly indeed, what are the general principles of Astrology, how they have to be mastered and with what restrictions they have to be applied. But in a small work of this nature it is not possible for him to explain anything more than the barest outlines of Astrology, and those who are conversant with the Sanskrit language will find what an amount of brevity could be introduced in the Stanzas relating to Sciences in Sanskrit. He takes <term>Sani</term><note>The planet Saturn</note> as the most prominent planet for inflicting death, as he owns the 10th and 11th houses but really <term>Buda</term><note>The planet Mercury</note> could have been named with an &c., afterwards. <term>Buda</term> owns the 3rd and 6th houses and consequently ranks as the foremost evil planet to a person born in <term>Mesha</term><note>The zodiac sign Aries</note>, because the two houses are bad and he is also a bad planet when his associations are evil. Why should <term>Sani</term> be then named?, is a question that we might easily ask the author. Perhaps if he were alive he would have answered thus his readers: <term>Sani</term> owns 10th and 11th houses, <term>Sani</term> is the lord of longevity and <term>Sani</term> has an inclination to kill persons in preference to other planets whether preceding or following him in his periods or sub-periods. We refer the readers to Stanza 28th and notes on it. 10th house owned by an evil planet is productive of good, and it is the last angle of the quadrant and therefore is considered the strongest. In the same way the owning of the 11th house by an evil or good planet is productive of evil and as 11th is the last house among the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 11th houses the fact of owning it confers the strongest tendency to produce evil. <term>Sani</term> is the *<term>Auyushkaraka</term>*<note>The significator or "causer" of longevity</note> or lord of life and therefore if he is well disposed he will increase the term of life and if ill disposed he will cut short the life. The influences of evil or good planets are of two kinds. First we have the general influence spreading among the different influences of the other planets and lasting all through the life of a person, this is called *<term>Nisargika</term>*<note>Natural or inherent influence</note> or permanent. Second, we have the influences of the planets in their periods, sub-periods, and other minor divisions of life. These are called *<term>Thathkalika</term>*<note>Temporal or "of the moment"</note> local or temporary. The latter influences last only during their respective divisions or sub-divisions. Here also the influences of the other planets cannot be overlooked. These two results of the planetary influences, must

As Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> has a special jurisdiction to protect life and take it away, his name has been inserted by the author in preference to others. If he is bad, it is only then that we have to give him the power to cut short the life. Otherwise the death-time must be determined in the period or sub-period of other planets. The author further says that it is necessary to reconcile the principles of Astrology whenever any difference is found among them and if the student is only intelligent and careful, he will see that practically there is no difference among the various systems advocated by the astrological writers.

### STANZA XLIV. Taurus.
<note>original: "Vrishabha"</note>

To the person born in the **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note> **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagnam"</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> and the Moon <note>original: "Chundra"</note> are evil planets; Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> and the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> are good planets. Saturn alone is capable of giving **Royal Success** <note>original: "Raja-yogam," a combination of planets leading to high status or power</note> to the person born in the **Taurus Ascendant**.

#### NOTES.

Jupiter is the lord of 8th and 11th houses both of which are declared bad. Venus is the lord of 1st and 6th houses and these are bad, as the first is a **quadrant** <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are the pillars of a horoscope</term>, and when owned by a **benefic** <term>a naturally "good" planet like Venus or Jupiter</term> the result is evil. Sixth house is equally bad, the Moon is the lord of the third house and it is bad. So that Jupiter, the Moon, and Venus are bad planets because they own 8th and 11th, 1st and 6th and 3rd houses respectively. These are bad planets and they always try to do evil to the native. The author then begins to tell us who are good planets for persons born in Taurus. The Sun owns the 4th house and as he is evil <note>naturally "malefic" or harsh</note> and owns one of the quadrants he becomes good and produces beneficial influences. Saturn is the lord of 9th and 10th houses and the possession of those two houses in one planet is excellent. Hence he becomes the most influential planet in the horoscope of the native born in Taurus and produces the greatest good and **Royal Success**.

<summary>This page explains the planetary functional nature for the Taurus ascendant, identifying Saturn as the most beneficial planet due to its dual lordship of the 9th and 10th houses. It also clarifies why naturally beneficial planets like Jupiter and Venus can act as "evil" influences for this specific sign.</summary>
<keywords>Taurus, Vrishabha, Saturn, Sani, Rajayogam, Royal Success, Ascendant, Lagnam, Planetary Lordship, Hindu Astrology</keywords>

### STANZA XLV.

> If evil planets like <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: "Guru"</note> etc., are possessed of death-inflicting powers, they will kill the native born in <term>Taurus</term> <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note> in their respective periods and sub-periods.

### NOTES.

<term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: "Guru"</note> now figures prominently as the death-inflicting lord to the person born while <term>Taurus</term> is rising at the time of birth. Why should <term>Jupiter</term> be named, and not other evil planets who have also been declared to be inauspicious as inflicting death? The <term>Moon</term> <note>original: "Chundra"</note> owns the third house and is bad. <term>Venus</term> <note>original: "Sukra"</note> owns the 1st and 6th houses, of which 6th is bad, while the first becomes bad by reason of its being owned by a <term>benefic</term> <note>A naturally "kind" or positive planet.</note> like <term>Venus</term>. He is besides the lord of the birth <note>The ruler of the rising sign or Lagna.</note> and has some consideration for the life of the person born under his own influence. <term>Jupiter</term> owns 8th and 11th houses and is the declared enemy of the lord of birth and has none of the redeeming points to alleviate the evil he is capable of doing to the person born in <term>Taurus</term>. He owns two evil houses while the <term>Moon</term> owns only one evil house; <term>Jupiter</term>, therefore, is stronger than the <term>Moon</term> and also <term>Venus</term> in producing evil. But in determining the evil or good which might result from the influences of the planets we cannot be too careful in weighing the evidence produced before us, and the facts have to be very nicely balanced before we pronounce our judgment in the matter. <term>Jupiter</term>, if well situated and aspected <note>Influenced by the "gaze" or position of other planets.</note>, may not inflict death. The student has to read well and digest facts.

### STANZAS. 47-48-49.

Mars <note>Kuja</note>, the Sun <note>Ravi</note> and the Moon <note>Chundra</note> are inauspicious. Venus <note>Sukra</note> alone is good. If Venus and Mercury <note>Buda</note> are well associated they give the "Yoga of Kings" <note>Rajayoga; a planetary combination indicating high status or success</note> (47).

When Saturn joins Jupiter, the result will be similar to what they produce on the person born in Aries. Saturn will not directly kill the person even when possessed of death-inflicting powers (48).

Mars and other evil planets become powerful in inflicting death. These results have to be carefully found out for persons born in Gemini <note>Mithuna</note> (49).

### NOTES.

In Stanza 46 the author says Mars and Jupiter are bad. Mars owns 6th and 11th houses. Both are bad for any planet to own. Jupiter is evil because he owns 7th and 10th <note>This refers to the "Evil of Quadrangles" where a natural benefic planet loses its goodness by owning certain houses; see notes to Stanza 6</note>.

# THE MOONLIGHT OF BIRTHS
<note>original: "Jatakachundrika"</note>

**Venus** <note>original: "Sukra"</note> owns the 5th and 12th houses and is a friend of the lord of **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note>, which is **Mercury** <note>original: "Buda"</note>; therefore, he is entirely good. **Saturn** <note>original: "Sani"</note> owns the 8th and 9th houses. The 8th is considered bad (*see page 9*), while the 9th is auspicious.

The combination of the lords of the **Angular houses** <note>term: Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and the **Trinal houses** <note>term: Thrikonas; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note> is productive of **Royal Union** <note>term: Rajayogam; a planetary combination indicating high status or success; see page 14</note>. The combination of **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> and Saturn produces no Royal Union for reasons well explained under the **Aries Ascendant** <note>original: "Mesha Lagna"; see Stanza 42</note>. In Stanzas 47-48-49, the author introduces the **Moon** <note>original: "Chundra"</note> as inauspicious. This introduction of the Moon as the lord of the 2nd house is called a **Difference of Opinion** <note>term: Matantara; a divergent school of thought</note>. The owning of the 2nd house has not been declared to be bad anywhere else by the author. The Moon and the **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note> each own only one house, while the rest of the five planets each own two houses. In stanza 7, the author says that the lords of the 2nd and 12th houses give good or bad results depending on whether their conjunction is good or bad and the nature of the houses they occupy. Then why should the Moon be called bad here, without knowing anything about his conjunction and the nature of the house he occupies? The Moon and Mercury are also not such inveterate enemies. 

The author anticipates two positions for the Moon. The two best houses for the Moon are **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note>, where he is exalted, and **Cancer** <note>original: "Kataka"</note>, which is his own sign. If the Moon is in Taurus, he will be in the 12th house from Gemini and is therefore bad. If he is in Cancer, he will be in a **Death-inflicting** <note>term: Maraka; a planet or house with the power to cause death</note> house, which is also bad. On these accounts, he must have been declared to be bad. Saturn will not kill the native himself even when he has death-inflicting powers. The reason is that he owns the 9th, a very good house. **Mars** <note>original: "Kuja"</note> and other evil planets kill the person. Mars owns the 6th and 11th houses, the Sun the 3rd, and Jupiter the 7th and 10th. All these are bad and enable their lords to kill the person born in Gemini. Jupiter certainly is bad if armed with death-inflicting powers. 

In the explanation of these points, it is not possible for me to take the reader into the labyrinth of the astrological formulas. It can only be pierced through by a little perseverance and energy. A few months' study will familiarize these apparently difficult points, and the formulas can easily be kept for ready reference by a well-balanced mind.

### STANZA 50—51—52. Cancer Rising.

Venus, Saturn, and Mercury are evil for persons born in <term>Kataka</term> (the zodiac sign of Cancer); Jupiter and Mars are also evil <note>The Sanskrit text suggests they are "known" as such in a specific technical sense, though Mars is simultaneously the primary benefactor</note>. Mars is alone capable of giving <term>Raja yoga</term> <note>original: "Raja yoga" — a planetary combination indicating high status, power, or "royal" success</note> (50).

The combination of Jupiter and Mars gives to the *Native* <term>Rajayoga</term> (Political success). The Sun does not kill himself although possessed of death-inflicting powers (51).

Venus and other inauspicious planets kill the person born in <term>Kataka</term>. We must judge of these results by the principles laid down in Astrology by the learned (52).

### NOTES.

Venus, Mercury and Saturn are evil. Jupiter and Mars are also evil. Mars alone is considered to be able to give <term>Rajayoga</term>. The combination of Mars and Jupiter produces <term>Rajayoga</term>. Here apparently there seems to be an error in the transcription of the original work. The statement that Mercury, Venus, and Saturn are evil to the *native* born in <term>Kataka</term>, is intelligible enough. Even Jupiter may be said to be evil, for he owns 6th and 9th <note>The 6th house relates to debts and enemies, while the 9th is usually fortunate; the mixture creates a complex influence</note> out of which the 6th house is bad. Mars owns the 5th and 10th houses and is the only auspicious planet for <term>Kataka Lagnam</term> <note>The "Rising Sign" or Ascendant in Cancer</note>. How could he produce evil when the two houses he owns are good and when he is the friend of the <term>Moon</term> <note>original: "Chundra"</note>, lord of <term>Kataka</term>? Of course if we go to the length of finding a far fetched cause in Mars being <term>Neecha</term> <note>Debilitated or at its weakest point</note> in <term>Kataka</term>—it may be imagined that he may not be able to do good. The originals with me contain *Dhishana Bhoosatou* <note>original: "Dhishana Bhoosatou" — referring to the planets Jupiter and Mars</note>—Which means <term>Guru</term> <note>Jupiter</note> and <term>Kuja</term> <note>Mars</note>. Any how in my humble opinion, I could not agree with the author if I am asked to read Mars as also being an evil planet. Lords of <term>Thrikonas</term> <note>Trines; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, considered very auspicious</note> are always good and Mars is the lord of the 5th house. Evil planets are good if they own <term>Kendras</term> <note>Quadrants; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and Mars is an evil planet. Jupiter owns two houses, the 6th and 9th, of which the last gives him good. The good given by the 9th house may be cancelled by the evil produced by the ownership of the 6th. When evil and good are equal or balancing we have to predict evil but in a mild form. There are other works which lay down the principle that when a planet owns one good and one evil house, he may be classed as a good planet perhaps in a mild form. This view is different from that of the author of this work and I have simply drawn the attention of the readers to such differences in opinions on astrological points. <term>Ravi</term> <note>The Sun</note> is the lord of the 2nd house and he will not kill the *native* <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note> himself. Why? perhaps he is the friend of <term>Chundra</term> <note>The Moon</note> and he does not like to get the blame himself. <term>Sukra</term> <note>Venus</note> and other evil planets when possessed of <term>Maraka</term> <note>Death-inflicting</note> powers, kill the person during their periods and sub-periods.

The 11th is bad and Venus owns it. <term>Sani</term> <note>Saturn</note> owns the 7th and 8th, of which the lordship of 7th gives him good while that of 8th produces evil. <term>Buda</term> <note>Mercury</note> is the lord of 3rd and 12th houses and he is necessarily bad. Cancer is an auspicious <term>Lagna</term> <note>Ascendant; the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> itself and when the Moon occupies it with Jupiter who is exalted here, great success will be produced. It is a watery sign and generally makes men stout and generous. The great Indian National hero Rama was born in <term>Kataka</term> <note>Cancer</note> with the Moon and Jupiter there, Saturn in <term>Thula</term> <note>Libra</note>, Mars in <term>Makara</term> <note>Capricorn</note>, Venus in <term>Meena</term> <note>Pisces</note>, the Sun in <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> and Mercury in <term>Vrishabha</term> <note>Taurus</note>.

He owns the 12th house from Leo <note>original: "Simha"</note>, and the Lord of the 12th is good or bad according to circumstances (*See* p. 8). The Moon is a friend of the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note>. My own humble view by long experience is that when the Moon is weak and is unfavourably situated there is nothing to prevent him from killing the party himself. However this opinion is modestly put forward from a general knowledge of astrology, and not warranted by any statement made by the author of this work. I am responsible for this view and I think when evil is predicted in the Moon's Period <note>original: "Chundra Dasa"</note> when he is weak etc.,—the prediction will surely be verified.

->Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon are evil; Venus alone is auspicious.<- <note>original: "Jeeva-shukrendavah papah eko-bhrigusutah-shubhah"</note>
->Mercury, when combined with Venus, produces the Yoga of Kings.<- <note>original: "Rajayoga-karas-saumyo bhrigu-putra-samanvitah"</note>
->Venus does not kill; others, such as Mars and the rest, are called killers.<- <note>original: "Nihanti-kavir-anyetu marakakhyah kujadayah"</note>
->The wise should thus deduce the evil and good results for those born in Virgo.<- <note>original: "Ghnanti papah shubhanyuhanyevam kanya bhuvo budhaih"</note>

## STANZA 56, 57. Virgo Ascendant <note>original: "Kanya Lagna"</note>.

Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note>, the Moon <note>original: "Chundra"</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> and Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> are evil to the person born in Virgo <note>original: "Kanya"</note>. Venus alone gives good. Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note> and Venus when in conjunction produce the Yoga of Kings <note>original: "Rajayoga"</note> (57). The Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> does not kill the person himself. Mars and others who are evil kill the *subject* <note>original: "native"</note>. In this way the learned judge of these results—good and bad—after careful consideration. (58).

### NOTES.

There are two expressions—*Kaviranyatie* (other than *Kavi* or Venus) and *Raviranyatie* (other than *Ravi* or the Sun). There seems to be some confusion in these two stanzas. How can Venus be called evil and again in the same breath be called good?

Jupiter is bad because he owns 4th and 7th houses <note>In astrology, owning two "angles" or Kendras can make a natural benefit planet act unfavorably</note>. The Moon is bad as he owns 11th house. Mars is not mentioned. The Stanza should begin *Kuja jeevandavahpapaha* <note>Mars, Jupiter, and Moon are evil</note> and then everything will be alright. Mars owns 3rd and 8th houses both of which are bad. Venus is good because he owns 2nd and 9th houses, and he is also called bad by

### NOTES.

**Saturn** <note>original: "Sani"</note> is bad, because he owns 3rd and 4th houses and is not friend of **Mars** <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, Lord of <term>Scorpio</term> <note>Vrischika: the eighth sign of the zodiac</note>. The ownership of the 3rd house is evil, while the ownership of the 4th by an evil planet is good. **Venus** <note>original: "Sukra"</note> is the Lord of the 7th and 12th houses, he is not a friend of **Mars** and therefore produces bad. **Mercury** <note>original: "Buda"</note> is the Lord of the 8th and 11th houses and both these houses are very bad to own for any planet. **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> is good because he owns the 2nd and 5th houses. 5th house is no doubt a very auspicious one to own; but the lord of the second need not always be good natured. The author does not say anything about the combination of the **Sun** and **Moon** (**Ravi** and **Chundra**). But we know as a matter of fact that the **Sun** and **Moon** are owners of each one house only. The **Moon** is the lord of the 9th a good house. The **Sun** is the lord of the 10th. The **Sun** is an evil planet and owns the 4th quadrant or 10th house and therefore produces good. The combination of the Lords of 9th and 10th houses must be productive of <term>Royal Success</term> <note>Rajayoga: a planetary combination indicating high status, power, or wealth</note>.

In many places the author of the *Moonlight on Astrology* seems to hint that the Lords of the death houses 2nd and 7th are not good and that when they have the power they are likely to kill the person unless they are relieved from this responsibility by owning some good house, by associations with good planets, and by favourable positions and aspects. Here we have an illustration of that principle. **Jupiter** is the lord of the 2nd house and he must produce death. But **Jupiter** also owns the 5th house and therefore becomes a good planet. All planets who own the 5th and 9th houses, whatever may be their nature become good and this goodness perhaps is the real cause of **Jupiter** saving the life of the person born in <term>Scorpio</term>. **Mercury** and other evil planets cause the death of the party. **Mercury** owns 8th and 11th houses and these are very unfavourable indeed for any planet to own. **Saturn** is the Lord of the 3rd house and he may kill.

### **STANZAS 63 and 64. Sagittarius Rising.**

To the person born in **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Dhanus"</note>, **Venus** <note>original: "Sukra"</note> is alone evil. The **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note> and **Mars** <note>original: "Kuja"</note> are productive of good. The combination of the **Sun** and **Mercury** <note>original: "Buda"</note> produces a **Royal Union** <term>Rajayogam: A combination of planets signifying high status, success, or political power</term>. **Saturn** <note>original: "Sani"</note> himself does not kill even when possessed of death-inflicting powers. (63)

**Venus** and other evil planets when invested with death-inflicting powers, kill the person born in **Sagittarius** and these results must be carefully predicted by the learned in astrology. (64)

### **NOTES.**

Sanskrit seems to be a very flexible language and one which can be suited to any interpretation. The first portion of Stanza 63 is the same in the different books we have but the latter portion differs considerably. *The combination of the Sun and Mercury* <note>original: "Yoga Bhaskara Soumyabyam"</note> is one reading while the other is *Joined with the Sun and Mars, the son of the Sun (Saturn) does not kill* <note>original: "Yukto Bhaskara Bhoumabhyam Nahanti Ravi Nandana"</note>. The first reading may be interpreted thus—the union or combination of the **Sun** and **Mercury** produces a **Royal Union**—

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA

success <note>original: "yogam"</note> while the other reading means that when Saturn <note>original: "Sani"</note> is in combination with the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note> and Mars <note>original: "Bhouma"</note>, he will not kill the person though possessed of death-inflicting powers. This point requires a thorough explanation. Venus <note>original: "Sukra"</note> is bad because he owns the 6th and 11th houses both of which are evil and we can at once see that Venus is bad. Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> and the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> are declared to be good.

Mars owns the 5th and 12th houses of which 5th is good and 12th bad, while personally Mars is a friend of Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> who is the lord of Sagittarius <note>original: "Dhanas"</note>. The Sun is both a friend of Jupiter and also owns the 9th house and thus he becomes a perfectly good planet. About the combination of Mercury <note>original: "Buda"</note> and the Sun one version is perfectly silent and I believe it is wrong or has apparently misunderstood the spirit of the author’s explanation of the astrological principles. We have it clearly stated by the author in the earlier portions of his work that the combinations of the Lords of the quadrants <term>quadrants: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, considered pillars of a chart</term> and trines <term>trines: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, considered the most auspicious</term> are always productive of good results and they give rise to Royal Success <note>original: "Rajayogam"</note> or success in political life. The Sun is the Lord of the 9th while Mercury is the Lord of the 7th and 10th houses both of which are quadrants. Therefore the combination of the Lords of the 9th and 10th houses must produce Royal Success. This has been omitted in the second reading and I believe it is an important omission considering the particular care the author has taken all along to show the existence of Royal Success whenever and wherever the Lords of the quadrants and trines unite together. The second reading, not only makes an important omission but also adds a misinterpretation. It says that the Son of the Sun <note>original: "Ravinandana," referring to Saturn</note> does not kill when he joins Mars or the Sun. It is opposed to the spirit of the author’s explanation. In none of the previous stanzas does the author say that a combination with another planet, exempts a planet from exercising such death-inflicting powers. Saturn, it must be remembered, owns the 2nd and 3rd houses of which the last is an unfavourable house. The owning of the 2nd has not been distinctly declared to be an inauspicious one. The author says here and there that its lord becomes powerful in inflicting death. Therefore we may safely stick up to the first reading which is more sensible and consistent with the author’s ways of explaining astrological principles. Who are other evil planets? Has not been clearly mentioned by him but those who are not men-

In stanza 64, there is another reading which also has to be carefully considered. The first reading, which is the most correct I believe, says <gloss>These results must be understood in this manner</gloss><note>original: "Gnathavyani Phalanyavan"</note>, while the second reading says <gloss>Mercury, when joined with evil planets, does not kill</gloss><note>original: "Budah-papayuthonathi"</note>. The first means that we have to understand the results above named in the manner described, while the second reading means that Mercury, when combined with evil planets, does not kill the person himself. I do not understand why Mercury should not kill when he is evil and when he possesses death-inflicting powers. The combination of an evil planet with other evil planets has never been stated to produce good, and death has been considered by mankind as the worst of evils—though this question, as a matter of fact, is open to serious objections into which it is not our present purpose to enter. 

These ill-considered alterations and erasures in the original books and manuscripts have been producing much injurious results, inasmuch as we have to deal with the correct spirit of the technical sciences and not with their philological or word-quibbling niceties. In the interpretation of a stanza, one has to be very careful about the explanation one offers. The spirit of the author must be preserved at any cost. His general principles of explanation must not be sacrificed for any vanity which might give the commentator some airs of originality which he wishes to assume; the explanations offered to the public must be consistent and sensible, so that they may, in no way, contradict the earlier portions of the work under reference and translation.

Stanzas 65, 66 and 67, *Makara Lagna—Capricorn*.
To the person born in <term>Makara</term> <note>The sign of Capricorn</note>, <term>Kuja</term> <note>Mars</note>, <term>Guru</term> <note>Jupiter</note>, and <term>Chundra</term> <note>The Moon</note> are evil; <term>Sukra</term> <note>Venus</note> and <term>Buda</term> <note>Mercury</note> are good. <term>Sukra</term> alone is productive of <term>Rajayogam</term> <note>A combination for high status or "Royal Fortune"</note>. (65).

In conjunction with <term>Chundratmaja</term> (<term>Buda</term>) there will result much happiness. <term>Sani</term> <note>Saturn</note> will not kill the person though possessed of death-inflicting powers. But <term>Kuja</term> and other evil planets, when they are invested with death-inflicting powers, kill the <term>native</term> <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note>. These are the results which are generally predicted by the learned about the persons born in <term>Makara Lagna</term>. (66 & 67).

Here there ought to have been some exception put in. The author stated in stanza 6 that when planets are evil and own <term>quadrants</term>, they produce good, while good planets owning <term>quadrants</term> produce evil. If <term>Chundra</term> is weak, debilitated or badly associated and aspected he becomes or is to be considered as an evil planet and therefore when he owns <term>quadrants</term> he must produce good. When aspected, he becomes auspicious and must produce evil: when he owns quadrants <note>In Vedic astrology, quadrants (Kendra) are the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>. <term>Sukra</term> <note>Venus</note> is good because he owns the 5th and 10th houses. In cases where a good planet owns trines <note>Trikona; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note> and quadrants together, he seems to lose the evil that he would otherwise produce as lord of a quadrant.

In conjunction with <term>Chundratmaja</term> <note>literally "Son of the Moon"</note> (<term>Buda</term> <note>Mercury</note>) there will result much happiness? This paragraph of the author is singularly vague. “Who should be in conjunction with <term>Buda</term>” is not clearly stated by the author; perhaps he means that the combination of <term>Sukra</term> and <term>Buda</term> produces <term>Rajayogam</term> <note>A "Royal Union" or combination for great success and status</note>. <term>Buda</term> is the lord of the 6th and 9th houses, of which the first is bad while the second is productive of good results. <term>Sukra</term> in this case does not want the help of anybody to produce <term>Rajayogam</term>. He is the lord of the 5th and 10th houses and he combines in himself the ownership of quadrants and trines. He has independent powers to confer good. No other combination could do <term>Buda</term> any good. <term>Chundra</term> <note>The Moon</note> is the lord of the 7th, a quadrant, and as such may produce good when he is weak or debilitated. To this there are powers added which ought to have made him a death-inflicting planet, but I suppose the owning of a quadrant coupled with the love he has for a person born under his own direct influence made him good to the person born in <term>Makara</term> <note>Capricorn</note>. 

<term>Kuja</term> <note>Mars</note> and other evil planets kill him. <term>Guru</term> <note>Jupiter</note> is evil, <term>Ravi</term> <note>The Sun</note> is evil as also <term>Chundra</term>. So that practically we have <term>Guru</term>, <term>Ravi</term>, <term>Kuja</term>, and <term>Chundra</term> for inflicting death, while <term>Sani</term>, <term>Buda</term>, and <term>Sukra</term> are for doing good, especially the last two planets. The author says that these results must be carefully analysed and understood by the students of astrology.

## NOTES.

<term>Sani</term> <note>Saturn</note>, <term>Sukra</term> <note>Venus</note>, <term>Ravi</term> <note>The Sun</note> and <term>Buda</term> <note>Mercury</note> are declared to be evil. Excepting the Sun, the other three planets are well known to be the foes of <term>Guru</term> <note>Jupiter</note>, the Lord of <term>Meena</term> <note>Pisces</note>. The Sun is his friend. But the Sun owns only one house and that is the 6th <note>The 6th house is traditionally associated with obstacles, debt, and disease</note>. Therefore the Lord of the 6th house cannot be good. <term>Sani</term> owns 11th and 12th houses and as such becomes perfectly evil, both houses being bad for any planet to own. <term>Sukra</term> owns 3rd and 8th houses; both are bad and Venus therefore produces evil. <term>Buda</term> owns 4th & 7th houses. If he is weak, badly associated or aspected, he becomes a bad planet and when an evil planet owns two <term>quadrants</term> <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, known as Kendras</note> he ought to produce good instead of evil. But when Mercury is well-aspected and is in conjunction with auspicious planets, he is said to become an auspicious planet himself and when he is considered a <term>benefic</term> <note>A naturally favorable planet</note> owning two quadrants he is bad and he must be considered in a similar manner. But no such explanations are offered by the author in the work under notice. <term>Chandra</term> <note>The Moon</note> is the lord of the 5th house and <term>Kuja</term> <note>Mars</note> is the lord of the 2nd and 9th houses and these are considered by him as productive of good results. Mars is not said to kill the person though possessed of death-inflicting powers because, although he owns the 2nd house, he is also the lord of the 9th which is a very good sign for any planet to own. The combination of Jupiter and Mars is needed for <term>Rajayogam</term> <note>A combination for great success, status, or "royal" prosperity</note>. Jupiter, it will be seen, is the lord of the 1st and 10th houses both being bad to own <note>According to the specific rules of this text regarding ownership of quadrants by natural benefics</note>. But the author has explained in the earlier portions of his work that for the production of Rajayogam the combination or junction of the lords of the 9th and 10th are needed. Saturn and other evil planets kill the person born in Pisces when they are possessed of death-inflicting powers. Saturn, Venus, Mercury, the Sun, and even Jupiter own evil houses and they may kill the person when they are invested with suitable powers for causing death to the native.

<header>THE MOONLIGHT OF NATAL ASTROLOGY.</header> <note>original: "JATAKACHUNDRIKA"</note>

## **STANZA 72.**

In this way, the results of the planetary combinations for the twelve Zodiacal signs must be understood by those learned in astrology. In a similar way, one should also explain the results of combinations for the significations of the twelve houses, commencing from the *Ascendant* <term>Lagna: the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>, whichever it might be and wherever we might commence it.

### **NOTES.**

The author first explained the general principles of astrology and then led us to the different results which we have to predict for each of the twelve Zodiacal signs commencing from *Aries* or *the Ram* <note>original: "Mesha"</note>. The significations of the twelve houses have not been explained by him here; they must be learned from other works on astrology. He further says that having known the results of the planetary combinations and lordships for the twelve houses, we must now proceed to consult in a similar way the elucidation of the good and bad which the native will have from the twelve significations commencing from the birth time or Ascendant. 

The birth time indicates the body, natural disposition, complexion, etc. The second house indicates eyes, wealth, family, speech, etc. The third indicates brothers and sisters, courage, maidservants, etc. The fourth denotes mother, education, happiness, conveyances, lands, etc. The fifth shows children, intelligence, councils, etc. The sixth declares debt, disease, misery, foes, etc. The seventh signifies wife, desire, potency, marital relations, etc. The eighth indicates death, longevity, etc. The ninth indicates father, religiousness, wealth, piety, etc. The tenth indicates reputation, success in political life, influence, etc. The eleventh denotes friends, elder brothers and sisters, influence, general success, etc. And the twelfth sign indicates sin, destruction, suffering, and expenditure. 

Thus it will be seen that each of the significations must be judged as if they were so many Ascendants, and the planetary combinations for them will have a special reference to their prosperity or destruction. On judging, for instance, of the second house, we have to see who are its lords, who are his friends, who are his enemies, what is the general attitude of the other planets towards this Ascendant, their strength and weakness, exaltations and debilitations, and various other sources of power which the signs as well as their lords are supposed to produce.

->If evil planets are in their debilitated state, or even if they are in their exalted state, what is the result?<-
->What will those planetary combinations do when the arrival of their own periods never occurs? || 73 ||<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "पापा नीचग्रहास्तेषामुच्चस्थे सति किं फलम् । तयोगाः किं करिष्यन्ति स्वदशानामागमे ॥ ७३ ॥"</note>

### STANZA. 73.

Planets which are <term>debilitated</term> <note>weakened by being in a specific zodiac sign</note>, or evil cannot do anything to the person when their own periods (<term>Dasas</term> <note>major planetary life-cycles</note>) do not happen or come. The same planets may be <term>exalted</term> <note>strengthened by being in a specific zodiac sign</note>, but what could they give?

### NOTES.

This is also singularly vague. The different copies I have do not agree in the readings and each may be separately interpreted. I shall however confine myself to the explanation of the stanza instead of quoting the different readings which will not much help the beginner in knowing the subject of <term>Astrology</term>. The planets may be good or bad, they may be exalted or debilitated, they may have good sources of drawing power or fruitful sources of commanding evil, but what will it avail if the periods of the planets do not happen to the person who is born under their influence. Take, for instance, an example. The total length of the periods of all the planets put together will give us a period of 120 years. They are distributed according to one system (<term>oodudasa</term> <note>the Vimshottari Dasa system based on the Moon's position</note>) as follows among the 9 planets. <term>Ravi</term> <note>The Sun</note>—6 years, <term>Chandra</term> <note>The Moon</note>—10, <term>Kuja</term> <note>Mars</note>—7, <term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon</note>—18, <term>Guru</term> <note>Jupiter</note>—16, <term>Sani</term> <note>Saturn</note>—19, <term>Buda</term> <note>Mercury</note>—17, <term>Kethu</term> <note>The South Node of the Moon</note>—7, and <term>Sukra</term> <note>Venus</note>—20. All these give a total of 120 years. A person is born in <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> and commences with the period or <term>Dasa</term> of the Moon. The period of the Sun is very good for the native and let me grant for argument's sake that the position of the Sun is also good and he is in every way powerful to do good to the native. But he is practically useless in as much as his period comes to the native after he has managed to live more than 114 years or so. And if his term of life extends to only 50 or 60 years, the period of the Sun never could do him any good at all because it never comes. It is something like this. Say I have an excellent friend who makes a promise to me that he would give me much wealth when he comes to me or when I go to him. If he never comes to me or I never reach him the promise remains practically a dead letter and I

<summary>This page explains that a planet's strength (exaltation or debilitation) is irrelevant if its specific time-period (Dasa) does not occur during the individual's actual lifespan. It uses the 120-year planetary cycle to illustrate how a beneficial Sun period might never be reached if it is scheduled too late in a person's life.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Jatakachundrika, specifically discussing the timing of planetary influences and the validity of certain manuscript interpretations regarding exalted and debilitated planets.</meta>

# JATAKACHUNDRIKA

will remain a poor man. Some other manuscripts say that when a planet is <term>debilitated</term> <note>A planet in its weakest zodiacal position</note> or evil, what could it do even when it is <term>exalted</term> <note>A planet in its strongest, most favorable zodiacal position</note> or good? This explanation does not seem to me satisfactory. The two conditions are absurd. In the first place, a good planet cannot become evil, and in the second place, an exalted planet cannot become debilitated; under these circumstances, how could they become good when they are quite the reverse? If the planets are bad or debilitated, the person need not fear anything when their periods do not happen to him. This sounds more sensible. I cannot entirely agree with the author in the above explanations. If a planet is exalted or debilitated, it is true he will not produce the best good or the worst evil until we get his period, and if that period never comes, then his exaltation or debilitation practically has no result for the person. But his sub-periods and other minor divisions must be occurring in the periods of other planets, and then it cannot be said that they will keep quiet. They may not give the person as much good as they could have given when they possessed the highest power for good or evil, but still as lords of the sub-periods etc., they will not remain inactive. They exercise their own influences and do so with some modifications. But of course, the author does not preclude us from attaching so much meaning. In a short stanza, he simply says that the periods of exalted or debilitated planets cannot do us good or evil when they do not happen to the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note> and thereby hints that much attention need not be paid to those periods in the writing of a horoscope as they do not come in time for enjoyment.

Influence of planets, it must be remembered, has been treated by the astrological writers under various heads:

(1) At the time of birth in the planetary combinations, each planet has got a general influence extending throughout life and subjected to certain local or temporary causes.

(2) The influences of <term>Dasas</term> <note>Major planetary periods in a person's life</note> or <term>Bhukties</term> <note>Sub-periods within a Dasa</note> exercised only during definite times and superseding as it were and considerably modifying the general influences.

(3) The influences of planets by their contact with or aspect by good or evil planets.

<meta>Digitized by Google watermark at the bottom of the page.</meta>

<summary>The commentator critiques various manuscript interpretations regarding the timing of planetary effects, arguing that even if a major period (Dasa) does not occur, sub-periods (Bhukties) still exert influence. He categorizes planetary influence into general life-long effects, specific time-bound periods, and influences gained through planetary relationships.</summary>

Now the author seems to have completely ignored the first kind of influence I have hinted. Suppose his major planetary period <note>original: "Dasa"</note> never comes. Does not the native derive substantial benefit from the fact of the Sun's elevation?

->When friends and enemies combine, the results are known to be mixed and ordinary.<-
->In quadrants, trines, or exaltations, when joined with friendly planets, the results are better. (Verse 74)<-

**STANZA—74.**

When friendly and unfriendly planets combine together they produce mixed and ordinary results. If the planets occupy <term>quadrants</term> <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope, considered pillars of strength</note>, <term>trines</term> <note>The 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, considered very auspicious</note> or places of <term>exaltations</term> <note>The specific zodiac sign where a planet reaches its maximum strength</note> in conjunction with friendly planets they produce better results.

### NOTES.

In the Astrological Self Instructor published by me (third Edition and also in my notes to the English translation of the <term>Gems of All Meanings</term> <note>original: "Sarvartha Chinthamani," a classic 13th-century astrological text</note>). I have clearly stated what is meant by friendship, enmity etc., among the planets. It is certainly not to be presumed that they will be constantly warring with each other as do the unprincipled and unbridled potentates of the earth, but the influences of the planets are to be calculated with reference to their rays which are constantly flowing from their different globes, crossing each other, counteracting against one another, augmenting or nullifying each others power. This is very important for the students to note. Enmity among planets means that when two rays shed by planets nullify each others influence they produce no perceptible results because each tries to counteract against the others influence. The combination of friendly and unfriendly planets produces very ordinary and mixed results. Unfriendly planets always try to influence the prospects of the native for bad while friendly planets try to produce good results. The person therefore gets good and evil results or what the world calls mixed results. The intensity is lost by the opposition and the extent of the

<summary>This page discusses how the combination of friendly and unfriendly planets creates mixed life results and explains planetary 'enmity' as the scientific interference of planetary rays.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary friendship, quadrants, trines, exaltation, Sarvartha Chinthamani, planetary rays, Jatakachundrika</keywords>
